- `4d082f928948911f` [general] SE-wide structured-output taxonomy (arXiv:2606.09395): 9 models × 4 structured SE tasks, zero-shot. Unconstrained: syntax+structural errors account for ~80.7% of all failures on JSON and function-calling tasks (LLaMA-3.1-8B emits 1,833 syntax errors on BFCL v2; Gemma2-9b 2,203), while coding tasks have <20% format failures. Grammar/regex enforcement cuts syntax errors to near-zero but improves task-level accuracy only modestly (CallNavi exact-match 0.4883→0.5130; Spider pass@1 0.6767→0.7113; BFCL v2 AST-match 0.1759→0.5274). MoE models gain almost nothing from decoder-side constraints (Qwen3-30B-A3B exact-match unchanged; Mixtral-8x7B collapses to 0.2363 under LLGuidance, worse than unconstrained) — logit masking cannot redirect committed expert routing. Usable grammars are scarce: of top-10 TIOBE languages only Python (Lark) and SQL (Lark) parsed a basic control-flow test. TTMG (template token-match generation) nearly eliminates syntax errors yet substantial structural and semantic errors persist — syntax enforcement is necessary but insufficient.

- `308c11e281ffbca4` [general] Two format-tax studies. (1) 'The Format Tax' (arXiv:2604.03616): 6 open-weight + 4 API models, structured output degrades every open-weight model (avg −5.8 to −8.7 pp); of 39 cells with significant effects, 36 (92%) are degraded by the format-requesting prompt alone, only 15 (38%) involve constrained decoding, 3 are GCD-only. GCD lifts format compliance 55.7%→92.2% with accuracy unchanged — the dominant cost enters at the prompt, not sampling bias. 2-Turn freeform-then-reformat recovers most loss (+6.8 pp avg, only 2/72 worsened ≈3% risk) at ~2x cost; thinking recovers more (+9.2 pp) but riskier (15% worsened). Newer closed models show near-zero tax. (2) 'Capacity, Not Format' (arXiv:2606.09410): with information-matched prose controls and 0% parse failures, JSON constraints are capacity-dependent — Sonnet absorbs them (p=0.25) but Haiku −36.2 pp and GPT-4o-mini −28.0 pp. Format-specific component −28.2 pp (Haiku), −19.3 pp (GPT-4o-mini); prompt-length component only −0.3 to −8.0 pp — the harm is premature serialization, not instruction length. Production-mode (GPT-4o-mini, MATH-Hard): CoT 60% → API JSON mode 51% → instruction-heavy JSON 34% → forced function calling 10% (−50 pp, models emit only tool-call arguments with no reasoning surface). Frontier immunity qualified: Opus 4.7 on AIME drops 96.2%→91.0%. Think-first-format-later recovers 79.5%/86.9% of the gap. Consensus: schema enforcement (decoding) is roughly cost-neutral on quality, but the format request itself is a real, capacity-dependent reasoning tax; forced-tool-calling is a production hazard on hard reasoning.

- `a4951002397ee060` [general] 'When Correct Isn't Usable' (arXiv:2605.02363) measured output accuracy = task-correctness × valid-JSON on strict {reasoning, answer} JSON contracts: NAIVE prompting reaches 77–85% task accuracy yet 0% output accuracy across all models/datasets; a minimal REFERENCE prompt still yields 0% for Gemma-2-9B and GPT-4o (both systematically wrap JSON in markdown fences). Constrained decoding (vLLM JSON grammar) enforces validity but at 3.6x–8.2x latency and can degrade task performance. Automated system-prompt optimization (AloLab black-box optimizer, meta-agent Claude Sonnet 4.5): GSM8K output accuracy 84.15/87.41/85.75%, MATH 34–40%, at 0.63–1.06x NAIVE latency; GPT-4o jumps 0% → 95.2% by adding a single 'no markdown fences' directive. Replacing the meta-agent with Claude 3 Haiku drops accuracy 84.15→61.0% (3/5 runs collapse) — optimizer capability, not the loop alone, drives reliability. Residual failure class is format-execution inconsistency (correct reasoning, wrong value in answer field) at 1.5–1.8% of failures.

- `97cf04e1f8130ba2` [general] The Constraint Tax on small models (Ray, arXiv:2605.26128; sub-3B SLMs, 15,000 generations): hard answer-only schema decoding raises schema validity 61.5%→100.0% but lowers answer accuracy 19.7%→11.0% (−8.7 pp) and raises wrong-valid-schema outputs 49.5%→88.9%. Per-model answer tax: Qwen2.5-0.5B −11.9 pp, Qwen2.5-1.5B −20.0 pp, SmolLM2-1.7B 0.0 (constraints help when they remove syntax failures without disrupting search). Production analogue: calendar tool-call task, prompt-only JSON 91.5% executable accuracy vs same object hard-encoded as schema 48.0% (−43.5 pp; 102/104 failures a single-field wrong duration_minutes — 'the error is semantic, not structural'); delayed-packaging control keeps 91.5% at 100% validity. Tax persists at 3B boundary (Qwen2.5-3B −15.3 pp). Backend-dependent: constrained decoding is a serving-path property, not model-only (SmolLM2 differs across vLLM/SGLang: 18.7 vs 25.7). Deployment rule: report schema validity, answer accuracy, executable accuracy, and wrong-valid-schema rate as a first-class reliability metric.

- `84c8cabac4c26d5f` [general] 'The I Don't Know Filter' (Broecker et al., UC Davis/Okareo, arXiv:2607.04034, verified in full text): abstention via a consistency-based filter is the cheapest repair (no second generation). Introduces IDKS = (C−I)/N (correct minus incorrect calls over total, abstentions neutral, −1..1), penalizing confident-but-wrong calls. Filter: run the agent K times (K=2..5), feed cross-run feature dispersion into a trained random forest, replace outputs above a threshold with 'I don't know'. Measured on three 3B-class SLMs (Llama-3.2-3B, Qwen2.5-3B, Phi-4-mini): IDKS gains often large — APIGen 0.342→0.541, Irrelevance 0.025→0.229, Parallel Multiple 0.320→0.490; whitebox features help most but marginal over gray ('the core uncertainty signal is largely captured at the output and probability levels'); Simple Java is a clear counterexample. Two failure modes: (1) confident incorrectness — low variance read as reliability (int-list call repeated on all five repeats, no signal to intervene, needs external type verification); (2) superficial formatting inconsistency — semantically-identical calls differing in surface form filtered as uncertain (remedy: canonicalize before feature extraction). Baseline IDKS declines as K rises, yet filter gains grow with K. Abstention gates must be paired with content-level checks, not variance alone.

- `f8edba98b002ec5d` [general] AgentDebugX (Zhu et al., UIUC/Toronto/Google/Stanford, arXiv:2607.18754, verified in full text): supplying the error location is the dominant lever for repair reliability — confirms the Tyen et al. principle ('LLMs cannot find reasoning errors, but can correct them given the error location'). Multi-turn root-cause agent DeepDebug (structure-guided reading, cross-examination, auditable report with responsible agent+step, evidence, one concrete fix; read-only over the trace): on Who&When (n=184) 56.0% responsible-agent accuracy vs 47.8% for best single-pass; strict agent-and-exact-step 28.8% vs 21.7%. On end-to-end recovery (GAIA validation, 165 tasks): localized fix repairs 13/73 failed tasks in one rerun vs 4/73 (CRITIC), 5/73 (AutoManual), 6/73 (Reflexion); overall 55.8→63.6% (+7.9 pp), largest gain on Level-2 multi-hop (48.8→61.6). Cost: ~5 model calls / 12.8K tokens vs 8.1K for one whole-trace pass (1.6x). Caveats: gain is model-dependent (a single global reading can be stronger on hosted backbones — motivating per-model routing), and swapping the structure-aware reading for a second global searcher costs 4.8 strict points on gpt-5.4-mini. Single toolkit, no third-party replication yet.

- `45e4bccf846fa0ba` [general] ATLAS-RTC (arXiv:2603.27905) is a closed-loop controller intercepting logits each step with a six-level graduated ladder (no-op → logit biasing → temperature modulation → token masking → mid-step KV-cache rollback), masking only at structural decision points and leaving value-generation zones free (intervening during value generation 'corrupts output'). On Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct: structured JSON under ambiguous prompts, first-attempt schema validity 56.7%→76.7% (+20.0 pp) with average latency falling 88% on the hard task (5,498→663 ms); agent tool-call first-attempt success 20.6%→58.3%, search tool 0.0%→96.7%. Rollback rare (~0.8 corrections/run, max depth 3 tokens). It enforces structure, not semantics: on send_email (missing required field) first-attempt success drops 16.7→0.0 pp; degrades on multiline string schemas and when baseline already saturates.

- `d84e1b455ff7763a` [repair] 'Do LLMs Know They Are Being Tested?' (Ahmed et al., arXiv:2510.08624; single-model study, GPT-OSS-20B, six paired A/B scenarios holding task content and decoding fixed): evaluation framing inflates chain-of-thought length (hundreds to >1000 chars) and reduces answer-only compliance; 'in structured outputs, it improves wrappers (e.g., fenced blocks, enumerated lists) but not regex-validated substance' — a direct instance of specification gaming (e.g., a fenced code block present while the regex-validated fix is wrong); incentive wording reweights the error mix; implication: benchmark prompts demanding strict formatting can inflate wrapper-level schema compliance without improving substantive value correctness — the framework, not the model ranking, is the contribution.

- `61d5bd996f9f2ad9` [general] 'Two Calls Beat Five Agents' (Prajapati & Mohite, arXiv:2607.26922, verified in full text): structured JSON inter-role communication degrades small-model multi-agent pipelines — Parishad five-role pipeline on Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct dropped GSM8K from 75.0% (direct) to 45.0% and HumanEval from 96.3% to 56.0%; a 7B model produces malformed JSON 30-40% of the time under a complex system prompt, and the parse-failure fallback (pass raw text downstream) causes error accumulation. Switching only the communication format to plain text (no architecture/model change) restored GSM8K to 82.0% and HumanEval to 81.7% (+37 points). On repair: two-call self-refinement beat the pipeline on math (86.2% at 7.4x fewer tokens) but the same ungated implementation destroyed HumanEval (Call-1 95.1% → 66.5%, fixing 4 and breaking 51) because the forced-rewrite Call-2 prompt rewrites working code; a task-aware gate (skip Call-2 on complete code blocks; conservative 'respond CORRECT if no bug') restored 95.1% (skips 163/164). Ablation: no-gate + non-coercive prompt = 96.3% (better than gated); gate + force-rewrite = 94.5% — the forced-rewrite instruction, not the gate, caused the regression. Authors' deployment rule: start from direct prompts; add self-refinement only when direct accuracy <85%; never use structured JSON communication in multi-step pipelines for sub-10B models.

- `b023dfa34d7b6603` [general] Dollar/token cost of retries — four independent measurements: (1) Token Budgets (arXiv:2606.04056) catalogs 63 confirmed production budget-overrun incidents across 21 frameworks (2023–2026), noting a single retry loop can spend thousands of dollars before an operator notices; central failure mode is delegation-fanout race. (2) Runtime-Structured Task Decomposition (arXiv:2605.15425): rerunning only the failed subtask cut retry cost to 436±132 tokens vs 1,632±145 static decomposition / 904±17 monolithic — up to 51.7% lower than monolithic, 73.2% lower than static. (3) Communication-Theoretic framework (arXiv:2605.09121): a cost-aware router achieves ~56% lower normalized cost than the strongest fixed technique at matched quality; contractivity shows generator-critic refinement turns contractive→divergent between 3B and 14B. (4) Agentic energy accounting (arXiv:2605.22883): agentic workflows consume 4.33x mean energy per successful goal vs linear baselines (888.1 J vs 205.3 J), driven by orchestration/retry structure, not inference compute. A 'retry loop' can be a $thousands incident or a ~50% cost reduction depending on where you retry and whether you aggregate.

- `2df44a2e25263275` [general] Locally constrained decoding (LCD — the Outlines/XGrammar/OpenAI stack) is distribution-biased and does not guarantee constraint satisfaction within a finite token budget (Dang et al., arXiv:2606.01926). Bias: LCD masks only immediately-violating tokens so renormalization deviates from the target conditional distribution (toy example: uniform p_lm over 3 bits with 'exactly one 1' gives LCD p=1/4,1/4,1/2 vs true 1/3 each). No finite-budget guarantee: a decoder can emit locally-valid unmatched '{' forever. Globally Constrained Decoding (GCD) masks with budget-aware criterion 𝒳_{t,n} guaranteeing validity at termination; P-GCD adds a distilled HMM. Measured (xLAM function calling, Llama-3.1-8B): constraint satisfaction at fixed budgets LCD 95.26–99.20% vs GCD/P-GCD 100.0%; accuracy LCD peaks 69.7% on xLAM JSON regardless of particles vs P-GCD 70.5–71.6%; P-GCD at k=4 (67.9%) beats LCD at k=8 (67.2%) on Spider. Cost: LCD 1.08x, GCD 1.10x unconstrained wall-clock, but P-GCD 3.11x–10.2x slower (5.26/8.23/17.30 ms/token at H=1024/4096/16384) plus one-time HMM distillation (~2 GPU-hours). Working on NFAs matters: Spider SQL char-level NFA 7,999 states vs DFA 57,263; DFA grows exponentially for JSON anyOf.

- `15b82401938c5a93` [repair] Palabrita (Oliveira, arXiv:2604.24636; on-device SLM practitioner case study, commit-level evidence trail 204 commits/5 days): five on-device failure categories with measured rates — F1 format violations (markdown code-fence wrapping majority of early calls, dropped to ~20–30% after 'no code fences' prompt; a structural parser needed for 15–20% of Qwen3 0.6B parses; JSON keys translated into prompt language; UTF-8 replacement-char corruption <5% but fatal), F2 constraint violations (wrong word length 30–50% of early generations — the 600M model cannot reliably count characters — dropped to ~10–15% after prompt hardening but 'still too high for production reliability'), F3 context quality degradation after ~3 generations (Qwen3 0.6B) / ~5–7 (Gemma 4 E2B) due to limited on-device KV cache, F4 latency (15–30 s per puzzle at 30–35 tok/s CPU), F5 model selection instability (6 models each needing a different prompt profile; cut to 2).

- `a090ad17666f0913` [general] 'Amplitude-Only FFN Intervention for Tool-Structured LLM Inference' (Xu et al., arXiv:2607.11183, abstract-level — PDF only, no HTML): inference-time FFN intervention as a repair route needing no retraining; a previous direction-changing intervention (Orthogonal Residual Projection) 'produced more regressions than repairs'. Amplitude Gating preserves FFN weight directions, modulates activation magnitudes, with a gated candidate-generation → ranking → acceptance/fallback pipeline and paired Per-Sample Fix-Harm Evaluation (PFHE). Exploratory offline mixed selector raises Qwen3.5-9B tool-route micro-average 38.66% → 42.92% (+4.27 pp); two Hermes function-call endpoints improve +7.64 and +7.62 points. PFHE view over 2,556 units: 48 fixes vs 26 harms (≈1.85:1 fix:harm), 294 preserved-correct, 2,188 preserved-wrong — even a successful intervention harms ~1 case for every ~2 it fixes. Authors report negative transfer across models/endpoints ('retain oracle headroom but no positive train-selected fixed tool route'); their conclusion: 'results support model- and task-specific selection with strict fallback, not a universal AG switch'. Numbers come from the abstract text; confidence in fix/harm counts high as stated directly in the abstract.

- `cfa462ba820abdc5` [general] PromptPort (Kotte, arXiv:2601.06151) formalizes cross-model "format collapse": structured extraction fails in production not because models lack understanding but because output formatting is unreliable across models/prompts — the same prompt returns clean JSON on GPT-4 but fenced/prose-wrapped/malformed output on Llama, so strict parsers reject otherwise-correct extractions. Introduces dual metric separating failure layers: ROS (strict parsing) vs CSS (post-canonicalization); on a 37,346-example camera-metadata benchmark across six model families it found severe format collapse (Gemma-2B ROS 0.116 vs CSS 0.246) and large cross-model portability gaps (0.4–0.6 F1). Recovery via deterministic canonicalization + DistilBERT verifier (safe-override, explicit abstention) recovers +6–8 F1, verifier-driven selection adds +14–16 F1, approaching per-field oracle (0.890 vs 0.896 zero-shot) without modifying base models, generalizing to held-out families.

- `1ab99428a950f6a4` [general] 'The Extrapolation Cliff in On-Policy Distillation of Near-Deterministic Structured Outputs' (Li et al., NTU, arXiv:2605.08737, verified in full text): on-policy distillation with reward extrapolation (λ>1) silently collapses the structured-output contract above a closed-form threshold λ*(p,b,c) (teacher modal probability, warm-start mass, IS-clip strength). Measured on Amazon Fashion K=8 listwise-JSON (Qwen3 1.7B→4B): parse rate 94.8% at λ=1.15 → 65.1% at λ=1.25 → 8.5% at λ=1.50; cliff localizes inside the predicted [1.18,1.28] bracket (5-seed CI [1.204,1.228]). NDCG@1 on parsed outputs is statistically flat across the sweep (p=0.61) — the λ effect is concentrated in format-adherence, not ranking quality. Operating just below λ* lifts a 1.7B student to 94.8% parse / useful=0.86, matching an 8B-SFT baseline at one-fifth the parameters. Decoder constraints add only +0.009 parse over unconstrained ListOPD's 0.943. Training-side and decoder-side fixes are complementary; parse-only evaluation can hide the collapse because semantic quality stays flat while the contract breaks.

- `473e91ddc0c06971` [general] CRANE (arXiv:2502.09061, ICML 2025) explains the format tax theoretically: constraining outputs to grammars that only allow syntactically valid final answers reduces reasoning expressivity (constant-layer LLM under such a grammar can only solve logspace-uniform TC0 problems; st-connectivity/NL-complete problems become unsolvable unless TC0=NL). Augmenting the output grammar with reasoning-permitting rules (Ga→R·G) preserves reasoning while keeping the final answer in the original grammar. CRANE decoding outperforms both constrained and unconstrained decoding: Qwen2.5-Math-7B 29% (constrained/CoT) → 38% CRANE; DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Llama-8B 21% → 31%; QwQ-32B 43% → 46%, with 100% parse where unconstrained CoT had ~10% syntax errors.

- `824c39e752901ec2` [repair] SLM survey claim (Sharma & Mehta, arXiv:2510.03847; abstract-level only, 9-page PDF not retrievable as HTML): SLMs (1–12B, sometimes up to 20B) 'are sufficient and often superior for agentic workloads where the objective is schema- and API-constrained accuracy rather than open-ended generation', with guided decoding + strict JSON Schema outputs + validator-first tool execution 'often lett[ing] SLMs match or surpass LLMs on tool use, function calling, and RAG at 10x–100x lower token cost'; proposes the reliability metric set cost per successful task (CPS), schema validity rate, executable call rate, p50/p95 latency, energy per request.

- `93d3f6c1ebd2262f` [general] Self-Refine (Madaan et al., arXiv:2303.17651, verified in full text): a single LLM as generator+critic+refiner improved performance ~20% absolute on average across 7 tasks (GPT-3.5/ChatGPT/GPT-4), but the gains are highly uneven: math reasoning gains are essentially zero (GSM8K GPT-3.5 64.1→64.1, ChatGPT 74.8→75.0, GPT-4 92.9→93.1) because the model cannot reliably identify errors (ChatGPT's feedback is 'everything looks good' on 94% of instances); gains appear (5%+) only when an external source flags incorrectness. Preference/constrained tasks gained 20-49 pp (Dialogue Response GPT-4 25.4→74.6; Constrained Generation 15.0→45.0). Iteration curves show diminishing returns (Constrained Generation 29.0→40.3→46.7→49.7). Feedback quality matters (specific 43.2 vs generic 31.2 vs none 0 on Sentiment Reversal). Of self-refine failures: 61% from feedback suggesting an inappropriate fix, 33% from wrong error location, 6% from refiner misapplying good feedback.

- `e88eb9f15941f838` [general] Constrained-decoding compliance (JSONSchemaBench Table 4; open engines on same Llama-3.2-1B backend, OpenAI/Gemini on proprietary models — cross-comparison explicitly 'not directly comparable'): GlaiveAI function-calling compliance LM-only 0.90, Guidance 0.98, Llama.cpp 0.97, Outlines 0.96, XGrammar 0.93, OpenAI 1.00 (declared coverage 0.89), Gemini 1.00 (declared 0.86). GitHub-Hard: LM-only 0.13, Guidance 0.69, Llama.cpp 0.63, XGrammar 0.41, Outlines 0.06, OpenAI 1.00 but declared coverage only 0.09. Kubernetes: Guidance 0.92, Outlines 0.58, OpenAI 1.00 but declared 0.21. JSONSchemaStore: Guidance 0.88, Llama.cpp 0.69, XGrammar 0.43, Outlines 0.24, OpenAI 1.00 but declared 0.06. Pattern: closed providers show near-perfect compliance within a small declared subset (reject/error on 70–94% of real-world schemas); open grammar engines cover more schemas with lower per-schema compliance (31–77% on hard suites). Compliance is only guaranteed on the subset of JSON Schema features the engine implements.

- `4cb71d0255cccf49` [general] Constraint Tax (Li et al., arXiv:2606.25605): when tool calling and JSON-Schema constraints are enabled together (schema compiled to grammar/FSM mask), all six tested open-weight models (Qwen3.6-35B, Qwen3.5-122B/397B, Qwen3-VL-235B, GPT-OSS-20B, Nemotron-3-Super) dropped Tool Invocation Rate from 100% to 0% (complete suppression), while closed GPT-5.4-mini stayed 100%. Robust across schema complexity, tool_choice='required', and frameworks (SGLang and vLLM identical). Root cause is mechanism-level: for Qwen XML-style tool format the leading '<' is not part of any valid JSON FSM state, so tool-call tokens are masked by design. Mitigation (Transparent Two-Pass Execution): TIR 0%→100%, JSON compliance unchanged, end-to-end success 0%→100%, at cost of one extra LLM round + tool-output context. Also cites Ray 2024: strict schemas raise format compliance but can lower answer accuracy.

- `5f7aa50430ec256f` [repair] Format request is a capacity-dependent reasoning tax (measured): the format request itself (not decoding) drives most loss — Haiku −36.2 pp, GPT-4o-mini −28.0 pp, Opus 4.7 AIME −5.3 pp, and forced function calling collapses hard-reasoning to 10%; think-first-format-later recovers 80–87%; three measured remedies: reasoning-augmented grammars (CRANE, +10 pp), trigger-gated single-call decoding (In-Writing, +27% on classification, though small models can emit the trigger prematurely and lose reasoning — a documented failure mode, so trigger choice matters), and prompt-level decoupling (two-pass +6.8 pp / thinking +9.2 pp).
  links: 4c19d9b8fed90b2c, e88eb9f15941f838, e2c28a05b7ee21b1, 2df44a2e25263275, 4cb71d0255cccf49, 473e91ddc0c06971, 1d482a9b4839f28a, 4626517e46b1c8e3, a4951002397ee060, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, 4d082f928948911f, 9c53a0de2e7964bf, 22afd8a8bede9bbe, 308c11e281ffbca4, 0f3c6731224b1b90, 8eb9a877c6a47673, 1d39a325632460a2, 7263566e1ca0c610, 605232ab8cd262a4, 7724947e09734d4a, 61d5bd996f9f2ad9, f3b751d3cfdaee27, 824c39e752901ec2, f26ea5ec230f7ffb, 457ddfbc8482528c, 998dc6a4eeebfd10, 01f00e1e3835a627, ef69e42494b930cc, 4b26f96529333302
- `6eb2a6b0a6c49c71` [general] Constrained decoding slightly helps reasoning task accuracy vs unconstrained (Llama-3.1-8B, Table 8): GSM8K 80.1% → 83.8% (Guidance), 83.7% (XGrammar); Last-Letters 50.7% → 54.0%; Shuffle-Objects 52.6% → 55.9%. But the TOON study (Matveev, arXiv:2603.03306; 21 models, up to 3 repair cycles) found plain JSON generation shows the best one-shot and final accuracy even vs constrained decoding, whose only significant advantage is lowest token usage. Mechanism: constrained decoding acts as a guardrail for weak models (Qwen2.5-Coder-7B-fast: 0% plain-JSON one-shot at 37,705 tokens → 75% under JSO at 4,440 tokens) but degrades strong models (Hermes-4-405B 92.5% plain → 35.0% constrained); JSO is the token-efficiency winner on simple tabular data. The measured quality effect of enforcement is small and capacity-directional.
  links: 6977145a51d83491, 74a333f06b0e1b1b, 888b4004f537880f, e88eb9f15941f838, 73c291787a0887ce, 18005e6da326e5fc, ef6a82f7cb4bf553, e2c28a05b7ee21b1, 2df44a2e25263275, 9f7bbdd4b6a5377b, 473e91ddc0c06971, 1d482a9b4839f28a, 4626517e46b1c8e3, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, 4d082f928948911f, edfc9ea838f66df0, 9c53a0de2e7964bf, 308c11e281ffbca4, 7263566e1ca0c610, 93d3f6c1ebd2262f, 61d5bd996f9f2ad9, 635c8cbfd002fa86, 1150dfe53f759651, fe15f8a0ae73fa1f, 84c8cabac4c26d5f, 06cc8dfc000757fa, 293c648eb7e6dfe0, 998dc6a4eeebfd10, 0a6c3cc372c5cf77
- `8eb9a877c6a47673` [general] IFHierBench (arXiv:2607.27912) — nested output constraints: 600 prompts, constraint-tree depths d=0..3, deterministic checkers. 67.8% of format-bearing prompts in real apps (from 1,232 GitHub prompts) carry ≥1 nested scope — nested structured output is the norm. Even the strongest model only marginally exceeds 50% prompt-level accuracy (GPT-5.5 53.7%; Claude 42.8%; others 14.7–33.2%); every model loses 37–53 absolute points from d=0 to d=1 (the 'universal cliff at the very first level of nesting'); at d=3 only the two strongest retain >20%. Two failure signatures: per-leaf content slippage vs outer-container failure marking every descendant leaf N/A (whole-subtree structural failure requires re-parse + rerender, not value-level patching).

- `635c8cbfd002fa86` [general] 'LLMs Cannot Self-Correct Reasoning Yet' (Huang et al., ICLR 2024, arXiv:2310.01798, Tables 2-4): intrinsic self-correction (no external signal) consistently degraded accuracy while multiplying cost (1→3→5 calls): GPT-3.5 GSM8K 75.9→75.1→74.7, CommonSenseQA 75.8→38.1→41.8; GPT-4 GSM8K 95.5→91.5→89.0; Llama-2 GSM8K 62.0→43.5→36.5. With oracle labels (external signal) the same loop gains (GPT-3.5 GSM8K 75.9→84.3, GPT-4 95.5→97.5). Multi-agent debate at equal inference cost is worse than self-consistency (debate 9 responses 83.0 vs self-consistency 9 responses 88.2 on GSM8K). Prompt-design artifact: on CommonGen-Hard, Self-Refine's apparent gain (44.0→67.0) disappears with a strong initial prompt (plain prompting reaches 81.8, applying self-correction drops it to 75.1).

- `7724947e09734d4a` [general] 'The Bitter Lesson of Tool Calling' (arXiv:2608.06370): programmatic tool calling (PTC — model writes a typed-Python-stub script executed in a subprocess) matches or exceeds JSON tool calling in 11/14 models on a 309-entry BFCL v4 subset; GPT-5.6 gains +10.6 pp, but three older OpenAI models (GPT-4o, GPT-4.1, GPT-5.4-mini) fall 19.7–26.9 pp below their JSON baseline — a single mechanical failure (they emit literal \n escape sequences instead of real newlines, so the subprocess syntax-errors). PTC loses 14.1 pp on average in parallel categories but gains 10.0 pp in live_multiple. Chaining: PTC advantage scales with chain length, +18.8 pp at length ≥12. Parallelism: JSON tool calling drops calls outright above a model-specific fan-out threshold (Claude Sonnet 5: 100% enumeration at N≤70, drops to 75% at N=72, 0% at N=100, while PTC holds 100% at N=100). Context rot: JSON degrades −2.3 pp under 128-schema flooding, PTC stable/improves (+5.5 pp). Token crossover at fan-out N≈26. Limits: echo-return stubs measure argument serialization, not end-to-end tool-use correctness; ablation n small (31–52); BFCL v4 LLM-judge mode has ~20% evaluator-human misalignment.

- `0a6c3cc372c5cf77` [repair] Enforcement cost is implementation-determined (measured): with mask-cache + CPU/GPU overlap, XGrammar mask generation drops to <40 µs/token ('up to 100×' vs regex/CFG baselines, ~zero end-to-end overhead), though the fastest configuration can be the most permissive (XGrammar's highest under-constraint count on the JSON Schema Test Suite); parser-stack classification gives O(1) vocabulary-independent masks (PSC, 700×/30× mask speedups, e2e throughput ≈ unconstrained); vLLM JSON-grammar constrained decoding: 3.6–8.2× inference latency with Gemma 52.4% duplicate outputs.

- `4cf12f9a8fbae069` [general] GGC (Yang et al., NTU/A*STAR, arXiv:2607.28082, verified in full text): when-to-repair is a measured design lever — selective correction beats correct-all. On Text-to-SPARQL (MCQA, 31,570-test; fine-tuned Llama-3.2-3B): of 3,085 generated-query errors, 77.70% are semantic (executable-but-wrong) vs only 21.07% syntax errors — execution-based correction triggers (common retry-on-error pattern) miss ~78% of true failures. A binary risk classifier gate (RoBERTa): accuracy 98.95%, recall 92.11%, FPR 0.31%, triggering the corrector on only 9.31% of queries. End-to-end: Generator-only 90.23% → correct-all (Gen+Corr) 92.34% → selective (Gen+Gate+Corr) 98.33% query accuracy, with ~45% less inference time than correct-all — correcting everything costs 2x and lands 6 pp below selective correction because unnecessary rewriting corrupts originally-correct queries. Threshold-robust (τ 0.25-0.75). KG-execution feedback adds little (98.27→98.70).

- `8af8f2689671ac0a` [evidence-status] Cross-framework comparison: mini-agi (verify_command/verify_target exit-code gate, outcome.achieved as run's own claim, unverified without gate, disagrees -> exit 1 + gap held + judge-drift calibration); SWE-bench (FAIL_TO_PASS/PASS_TO_PASS in Docker, patch is the artifact, no self-report, wrong-but-green patches the open failure mode); Terminal-Bench/Harbor (test.sh -> reward file, separate verifier env, tamper-sensitive evidence via sidecars); Claude Code (user-supplied check, Stop hook blocks turn with 8-block override, fresh subagent refutes); OpenAI Evals (deterministic comparators, LLM grades own output, D/E labels); Voyager/Reflexion/LATS (self-critique is the loop's own feedback signal, no separate judge).

- `ec766cdefb66d42e` [general] SOB (Singh et al., arXiv:2604.25359; 21 models): best Value Accuracy 0.830 text, 0.672 images, 0.237 audio. Every model has JSON Pass ≥84%; JSON-Pass-vs-Value-Accuracy gap is a consistent 15–25 pp (GPT-5.4: 99.7% JSON Pass but only 48.6% Perfect Response). 'The scaffolding is correct; the content inside it is not.' Model size does not predict value accuracy (Phi-4 14B 0.798 > GPT-5 0.795). Structured-decoding ablation (audio): adding schema-constrained decoding moves Value Accuracy only −0.007 to +0.033 while JSON Pass rises for two and falls for GPT-5.4 (0.869→0.808) — the cleanest controlled evidence that enforcement improves structural reliability but not value correctness. Also cites Park et al. (2024): naive grammar-constrained decoding 'distorts the LLM's distribution toward syntactically valid but semantically degraded outputs'.

- `edfc9ea838f66df0` [general] Schema key wording is an instruction channel under constrained decoding (Le, arXiv:2604.14862): holding model/prompt/structure/decoding engine (XGrammar) fixed and varying only where the instruction signal lives, Qwen2.5-7B GSM8K 79.61→86.50 under Key-only (+6.89), Qwen2.5-Coder-14B best under Both (+4.93); but Llama-3.2-3B drops 53.15→37.38 under Key-only (−15.77) while Prompt-only/Both improve it, and Llama-3.1-8B shows 0.00 Key-only change. Qwen models read the schema as a task instruction; LLaMA models treat schema wording as distraction. Interaction non-additive: on Qwen2.5-7B Math500 'Both' falls below baseline (25.20 vs 27.80). Identical JSON structure with different key names can move accuracy by ±15 pp depending on model family.

- `1d482a9b4839f28a` [general] In-Writing / 'Thinking Before Constraining' (arXiv:2601.07525): single-call two-phase — model reasons unconstrained (state −1), structured decoding engages only after a trigger token (state 0). Beats the NL-to-Format two-step baseline on almost every task: GSM8K LLaMA 74.7→77.9, DDXPlus Gemma 22.9→50.1 (+27.2 pp), MultiFin Gemma 70→86.9, Sports LLaMA 69.5→77.4. Overhead only 10–20 extra tokens (~0.5–1.5 s). Caveats: premature trigger-token generation is a documented failure (small models emit '{' mid-reasoning; mitigation: use '<eos>' as unique trigger), and it is 'not a perfect parser' (Appendix A.4.1 parse failures).

- `7486a7f9f63098ee` [general] LLM-as-judge reliability limits: (1) Merge-conflict resolution judge (Shen, arXiv:2607.27674, Tables 1-4): an LLM judge calibrated against human labels (n=292) reached 100% precision (zero false accepts) at 64.6% recall (accuracy 78.1%), and the LLM judge accepted 4 of 5 resolutions that fail the deterministic structural check — 'structural correctness must not be delegated to an LLM'. The same study's solver (deterministic gates: parses, no leftover markers, no duplicate declarations; up to 4 retries feeding validator errors back) reaches 95.8% structural validity on true conflicts. (2) Counsel (Pisupati et al., arXiv:2606.21627, verified in full text): process-level LLM-judge critiques on τ-bench/DA-Code, human meta-evaluated (Krippendorff α≈0.78, 1,131 annotations); strongest judge ~88% agreement on error location but only ~65% on reasoning quality; best judge's critiques are 'spot on' only 58-63% of the time on τ-bench retail (Qwen3 63%, GPT-OSS-120B:high 58%) and 43-60% on DA-Code. When repair feedback is LLM-generated, the judge's own reliability caps the repair ceiling.

- `a2d0d1644fe1d3d4` [repair] Uncertain / not verifiable from reached sources: there is no single canonical 'JSON failure rate'; vendor guarantee statements (OpenAI, Anthropic) are first-party claims — OpenAI publishes one measured ladder (prompting <40% / trained-only 93% / strict 100%) on its own complex-schema eval and explicitly limits the guarantee to its supported JSON-Schema subset; whether closed-provider compliance stays ~100% outside that subset is unverified; the IFEval-FC tables, BFCL-robustness table, and production/system reports (TCMIIES, SQLQE, xmemory, ContinuityBench, AgentTether) are limited to specific models/domains at one point in time and mostly lack independent replication; τ-bench and SchemaBench headline numbers are for 2024-era model versions.

- `4626517e46b1c8e3` [general] Draft-Conditioned Constrained Decoding / DCCD (arXiv:2603.03305) formalizes constrained decoding as repeated reverse-KL projection onto the prefix-valid set; quality degradation is driven by low feasible mass — per-step distortion KL = log(1/α(h_t)); on a toy single-slot JSON the constraint admits ~1% of vocabulary. DCCD (unconstrained draft first, then constrained decoding conditioned on the draft) beats standard constrained decoding on every model×dataset: GSM8K 1B 15.24% → 39.04%, 1.5B 49.36→73.92 (largest gain), 7B 81.58→91.28, 14B 86.43→95.15; fixes GSM-Symbolic where CD collapses to 0.0 at 1B. A 1.5B+1.5B DCCD pair scores 12.7 accuracy-per-billion on MATH500 vs 3.6 for 8B CD (~253% better); 7B+1.5B beats a single 14B CD on GSM8K with 39% fewer parameters. Sampling+voting drafts scales better than sampling under hard constraints, saturating around n≈7.

- `ef69e42494b930cc` [repair] Repair mechanics are measured at the mechanism level: supplying the error location is the dominant lever — attribution-based repair fixes 13/73 GAIA tasks vs 4–6 for decoupled self-correction, strict agent+step attribution 28.8% vs 21.7% single-pass (AgentDebugX); procedure-directed retry corrects 84.6% of UNSAT failures where a generic retry corrects 11.5% (Tell-Tale Trace, Llama3-70B, +73.1 pp); a consistency-based abstention filter raises function-calling IDKS 0.342→0.541 (APIGen) at the price of confident-incorrectness blind spots (5/5 identical wrong calls) and formatting-variation failures (IDK Filter); inference-time FFN gating repairs at ~1.85:1 fixes:harm with model-specific transfer (Amplitude Gating, PFHE 48 fixes/26 harms/2,556 units).

- `f103d9ef613d250f` [general] Synthesis across §5 (repair patterns): repair loops are only reliably productive when an external, trustworthy signal (tests, oracle labels, a stronger model, a human) drives them; purely intrinsic repair reliably degrades correctness. The one measured intrinsic-but-external-verified exception is cross-episode repair memory (MERIT): +3.5 pp (Spider), driven mainly by schema-local episodic experience, at token overhead and without beating untyped retrieval. Correcting everything is worse than correcting selectively (GGC: routing ~9.3% of queries through a corrector reached 98.33% vs 92.34% correct-all at 45% less inference time — unneeded correction is itself a failure source, so a gate deciding when to repair is as important as the repair itself). For structured-JSON output the reliable repair equivalent is the deterministic verifier + retry/regenerate loop.

- `f4e7be8b62f08af8` [general] VeriHarness (Ray & Goyal, arXiv:2607.14167, verified in full text): feedback content determines repair success in a generate-validate-retry loop (50 paired TextWorld games, four-call cap). Feedback carrying failure location, observed value, and admissible alternatives raised terminal success from 14/50 to 36/50 for Qwen2.5-Coder-14B (+44 pp, CI 28-60, p=3.15e-5) and 8/50 to 29/50 for Llama-3.1-8B (+42 pp). Ablation: prose with same repair values gains the same +42 pp; TypedFields (keyed JSON record + label) differs from matched prose by only +2 pp (Qwen)/0 (Llama), p=1.0 both — 'no evidence that JSON syntax itself improves repair'; admissible alternatives account for most of the gain (+36/+40 pp) since LocObs (location+value, no alternatives) stays at baseline. Extra calls only help when the retry receives new information; raw-diagnostic retries are flat from 4 to 8 calls while structured feedback keeps gaining. Typed feedback uses fewer calls and 14%/10% fewer prompt words.

- `a4d77e88c7e61873` [general] ScrapeGraphAI-100k (arXiv:2602.15189) — the largest real-world schema-constrained corpus: 93,695 production telemetry events, 18,000+ unique schemas. Average raw-corpus schema-validity ~93% but validation falls sharply at structural breakpoints: depth ≥7 drops validity 98%→20%; key count ≥200 drops to ~65%; composite complexity >1,000 drops to ~55%. Key count alone is a sufficient complexity proxy (metrics multicollinear, r≥0.98). 87.4% of requests came from GPT-4o-mini (behavior biased toward one model); validation measures only syntactic compliance, not semantic correctness. Distillation (Qwen3-1.7B, GPT-5-nano teacher): schema compliance 0.7954→0.9167 but value score stays 0.4624 vs 30B's 0.4797 — 'correctly identifying which fields to extract is easier than accurately extracting what those fields contain'.

- `162dbf907c0f473e` [general] Diffusion LLMs: structured-output collapse under stochastic sampling (Dang & Ermon, arXiv:2607.07026). Switching greedy→stochastic destroys unconstrained dLLM structured accuracy: Dream BFCL-Live (JSON) 63.9% → 22.3%; format gap is structural not semantic (Dream BFCL-Live JSON 63.9% → Python 22.4%). Exact constrained decoding over finite automata (chain graphical model, guaranteed 100% constraint satisfaction, log-depth tree sampler) recovers: BFCL-Live stochastic 22.3→69.0, Python 22.4→69.7, Sudoku 36.4→92.4, Countdown 53.6→100, at only +4% wall-clock (chain variant +114%). Ablation: recomputing remasking confidence under constrained marginals beats mean-field. Limitation: finite automata only, not CFGs.

- `22abb140e3c15e39` [general] 'Reinforced Agent' (Ta, Zhu, Shayandeh, Apple, arXiv:2604.27233, verified in full text): a separate reviewer agent evaluating provisional tool calls before execution (progressive-feedback loop plus best-of-N variants), base GPT-4o, on BFCL and τ²-bench. Helpfulness–Harmfulness decomposition: o3-mini corrects 36.8% of errors while breaking 11.7% of correct cases (3.1:1 benefit-to-risk) vs GPT-4o 34.9%/12.9% (2.7:1) — a good reviewer still breaks ~12% of correct tool calls. Net effects: BFCL irrelevance detection 84.9→90.4% (+5.5%), τ²-bench average 48.7→55.8% (+7.1%); progressive feedback beats best-of-N (+3-8%). τ² failure-mix shift: policy-constraint violations −13%, missing context-awareness −9%, but over-verbalization +17%. Latency is the binding constraint: 6.2x on single-turn BFCL (1.27→7.87 s) but only 2.4x on multi-turn τ² (amortized over ~40 turns) — a reviewer loop is ROI-positive only where errors are costly and latency amortizes. Requires a reasoning reviewer and always pays a ~12% harmfulness tax that a deterministic verifier does not.

- `07bdd35766db54d4` [repair] Enforcement guarantee is approximate (measured): the standard locally-constrained stack (Outlines/XGrammar/OpenAI) is distribution-biased (renormalizing over locally-valid tokens distorts sampling from p(x|constraint)) and can fail validity within a finite token budget — LCD constraint satisfaction 95.26–99.20% at fixed budgets vs 100% for budget-aware GCD (GCD ~1.1× latency; P-GCD probabilistic look-ahead 3.11–10.2×); on MoE architectures Outlines/LLGuidance/XGrammar give ~0 gain and Mixtral LLGuidance AST collapses 0.409→0.236; grammar scarcity is removable — learned CFGs from execution logs reach 0.91–1.00 precision vs 0.025 for formal induction (Autogrammar).

- `2cafe6061a29c299` [general] Parse-then-repair loops reach ~100% within scope: (1) FEniCS natural-language interface (Upadhyay & Reinhart, arXiv:2606.10928): 9/15 valid on first pass (60%), all remaining repaired after retry → 100.0% final valid parse rate, 100.0% problem-class and 97.1% field-extraction accuracy; one unrecovered invalid-geometry failure in a separate 10-case benchmark (90% final). (2) PCN-Rec (Dixit & Dixit, arXiv:2601.09771): verifier-accepts-only certificates with deterministic constrained-greedy repair raise constraint pass rate to 98.55% (feasible users, n=551) vs a one-shot no-verification baseline, at a 0.021 absolute NDCG@10 cost. Deterministic verifier + scoped repair closes most of the residual gap (to 98-100%) without closing the semantic-value gap.

- `90b87f746aa7223d` [general] ToolBench-X (arXiv:2606.25819) benchmarks task completion under recoverable tool-environment hazards (five injected types: Specification Drift, Invocation Error, Execution Failure, Output Drift, Cross-source Conflict; each instance solvable via a valid recovery path). Findings (abstract-level): agents that perform well with reliable tools often fail under recoverable hazards; failures are driven less by tool-use volume or inference budget than by limited hazard diagnosis and ineffective recovery; targeted recovery hints recover many failed tasks while test-time scaling yields more limited gains — recovery quality, not retry quantity, is the binding constraint when the tool environment misbehaves.

- `a5b5c576f4ffd0b5` [general] OpenAI's Structured Outputs launch post (2024-08-06, first-party vendor eval, not independently replicated): prompting alone <40% (gpt-4-0613), trained-only model 93% (gpt-4o-2024-08-06), 100% with strict constrained decoding. Guarantee holds only for a supported JSON-Schema subset, and 'Structured Outputs doesn't prevent all kinds of model mistakes'. One-time first-request cost: '<10 seconds... more complex schemas may take up to a minute' then cached. Shape is consistent with JSONSchemaBench's finding that closed-provider compliance is ~100% within their supported subset.

- `2bc3ff3548b14a6f` [general] Verdict synthesis: DMR (MemGPT, 2023) measures single-session-hop fact recall on short 5-session MSC chats and is near-saturated (full-context baseline matches the memory system); LoCoMo (2024) measures long-horizon QA across single/multi-hop, temporal, open-domain, and adversarial questions plus event-summarization, with temporal reasoning and abstention the hardest axes (human 87.9 F1 vs. best model ~37.8; long-context adversarial 2.1%); LongMemEval (ICLR 2025) explicitly measures IE, MR, TR, KU, ABS at 115k–1.5M-token scales, documenting 30–60% drops for long-context LLMs and 37–64% drops for commercial memory assistants.

- `7263566e1ca0c610` [general] 'Faithful, Not Corrective' (arXiv:2607.09678) — multi-hop relay format effects are tier-dependent: at the strong tier no telephone-game collapse occurs (every format retains QA recall ≥0.973 after 6 hops; loss concentrates in the first encoding step, free NL shows only a small drift slope −0.295); under a weak relay, across-format dispersion of hop-6 recall grows 8.7x, with an encoding toll paid by rigid formats (hop-1 Kv 0.765/Json 0.800 vs Constr 0.962) versus drift resistance of the fixed-key JSON schema (Json loses only 4.7 points over hops 1–6, retaining 0.94 of its hop-1-correct facts vs 0.74–0.85 for others). Once a wrong value is present it persists to the final hop in 83–100% of chains in every format with no collateral cascade — 'structure is a faithful, error-localizing channel, not an error-correcting code'. Choose the format for the weakest relay in the pipeline.
  links: 229361857af8028c, 0cae2c2bdf2ef1dc, 6eb2a6b0a6c49c71, a4951002397ee060, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, 45e4bccf846fa0ba, edfc9ea838f66df0, 9c53a0de2e7964bf, 308c11e281ffbca4, 8eb9a877c6a47673, 605232ab8cd262a4, 61d5bd996f9f2ad9, f8edba98b002ec5d, 4cf12f9a8fbae069, add1799958c47c93, a090ad17666f0913, d84e1b455ff7763a, 5f7aa50430ec256f, 952039c898e69791, 998dc6a4eeebfd10
- `cee7417852b29375` [general] Retry consistency (STED, arXiv:2512.23712): Claude-3.7-Sonnet shows near-perfect structural consistency 0.999±0.037 even at T=0.9 with only 19% degradation from T=0.1→0.9; Claude-3.5-Haiku is the most temperature-fragile (46% decline). Deployment rule: T=0.1–0.3 maximizes consistency, T≥0.7 causes >30% semantic drop. If a model is temperature-fragile, retries sample from a moving target and expected-draws math understates the true budget; format stays stable while content drifts — retry-on-schema alone won't converge content. StepCache (arXiv:2603.28795): selective patching of the failed region (step-level reuse + lightweight verification + patch-only regeneration) cut latency 2.13 s→0.67 s and tokens 36.1k→27.3k, raised end-to-end correctness 72.5%→100%; 79.7% of requests on reuse-only fast path.
  links: a4951002397ee060, 45e4bccf846fa0ba, 4d082f928948911f, 9c53a0de2e7964bf, a4d77e88c7e61873, 308c11e281ffbca4, ec766cdefb66d42e, 8eb9a877c6a47673, dc2abae1015a10fc, b023dfa34d7b6603, 4c4453fd5125f78d, f4e7be8b62f08af8, 7486a7f9f63098ee, 84c8cabac4c26d5f, 15b82401938c5a93, bea9df1d1fc5af19, 55dfb567f49f3e98, f4596f48ade2d79f, e03589b07f291e50, ce00079fe0159247
- `1d39a325632460a2` [general] TSCG (arXiv:2605.04107): JSON-schema volume causes a capability cliff for small models (4B–14B) — tool-calling accuracy collapses to 0–49% at >15 tools. A deterministic JSON→text schema compiler restores small models: Phi-4 14B 0% → 84.4% at 20 tools (90.3% at 50), Mistral 7B 35→80.1, Gemma 3 4B 49.9→67. The mechanism is format translation, not compression: R² of the accuracy predictor drops 0.88 → 0.03 when a plain-text baseline replaces JSON — almost the entire gain comes from writing the schema as text rather than JSON. On frontier models text also beats native function-calling API (mean +10.9 pp) while saving 6–50% tokens. Caveats: per-version operator inversion and super-additive negative operator interactions — no universal profile; aggressive compression harms strongly fine-tuned models. JSON (the universal production standard) is measurably the worst representation for small models.
  links: cfa462ba820abdc5, 4cb71d0255cccf49, 1d482a9b4839f28a, a4951002397ee060, 4d082f928948911f, 9c53a0de2e7964bf, 308c11e281ffbca4, 8eb9a877c6a47673, f15a4d58c0bb1a4a, 7724947e09734d4a, 4ca81b227fadc600, 61d5bd996f9f2ad9, 1ab99428a950f6a4, a090ad17666f0913, 15b82401938c5a93, 5f7aa50430ec256f, 457ddfbc8482528c, 998dc6a4eeebfd10, ef69e42494b930cc
- `4ca81b227fadc600` [general] CAPC (arXiv:2607.15516) — caching economics of long-schema workloads (Anthropic Sonnet 4.6): prompt cache is two-tier with a sharp threshold near 3,500 tokens (below: hit rate plateaus at 0.833 exactly; above: ρ=1.0 from first call). At compression ratio r≥6, cache-only never beats query-aware compression. CAPC (query-agnostic compression + explicit cache_control + tier-preserving ratio bound) is cheapest in 16/16 configs, mean savings 49% over cache-only, 64% over query-aware, 90% over vanilla, quality within 0.05. Production: 94k-token tools= schema prefix → 51.7% cost reduction at r=3. Over-compression failure: on a 12,191-token doc, r=3 keeps the prefix in the persistent tier ($0.0057/query) but r=4 pushes it to the hot tier and per-query cost rebounds 77% to $0.0101. Naive per-query prompt variants silently invalidate the cache.
  links: 7d97975df00109c5, 348e2784f9115e96, e286837b45d310e8, 1eb6cac2ee3c0b55, 53b44257c13294e1, e9faf4c799927644, 0b60fb3ea9f2562a, 7099cb2b4578c725, 4626517e46b1c8e3, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, 308c11e281ffbca4, 1d39a325632460a2, 7724947e09734d4a, b023dfa34d7b6603, 1ab99428a950f6a4, 4cf12f9a8fbae069, 86be403af727cc0f, 15b82401938c5a93, 01a2672e8349e37a
- `a8eddf3f8aca8331` [general] OrderBench (arXiv:2607.18261) quantifies the schema-valid-but-wrong gap: GPT-OSS-120B-fast achieves 100% JSON and schema validity yet semantic success 83.0% (prompt) / 81.3% (JSON-schema); Qwen3-30B-A3B 100% schema-valid with semantic success only 30.7–31.3% and unsafe acceptance ~14–15%; Gemma-2-2B yields 100% schema-valid objects with 2.0% semantic success and 41.7% unsafe acceptance. Category-level unsafe rates reach 51.7% (unavailable item) in schema-valid output. Paired comparison: JSON-schema mode reduces unsafe acceptance for weaker models but never eliminates it, and slightly lowers semantic success for the strongest (−1.7 pp). Deployment rule is a four-layer contract ending in fail-closed on verifier errors with clarification rather than auto-repair for safety-critical fields.

- `348e2784f9115e96` [general] On knowledge-update and temporal reasoning: LongMemEval authors report 'Naive time-agnostic memory designs perform poorly on temporal reasoning questions'; time-aware indexing/query expansion improves temporal-reasoning recall by 6.8–11.3%; KU questions require retrieving BOTH pre-update and post-update evidence, with a store that drops the older fact counted as failed retrieval.

- `01f00e1e3835a627` [repair] Serving-stack corruption (measured): a routine sign-branched repetition-penalty sampler setting (θ=1.3) drops JSON schema compliance 97%→23% on 200 real schemas (58–96% of greedy tokens change; gauge-dependent per model) — a corruption neither constrained decoding nor retries recover; diffusion-LLM structured output collapses under stochastic sampling (Dream BFCL-Live 63.9→22.3%) and recovers only under exact FA-constrained decoding (22.3→69.0% at 100% CS, +4% overhead).

- `888b4004f537880f` [general] Renze & Guven (FLLM 2024) tested 9 LLMs × 8 self-reflection agent designs on a 1,000-question multi-domain MCQA exam and found all reflection types significantly improved re-answer accuracy (p<0.001) across all models, with information-rich reflections (Instructions, Explanation, Solution) beating minimal ones (Retry, Keywords, Advice), and even the plain 'Retry' agent improving. Protocol caveats from the paper itself: reflections were generated with the correct answer given as an external feedback signal (answer labels redacted afterwards), the task is single-step re-answering rather than an agent loop, the LSAT-AR exam dominated the gains, and strong models near the 100% ceiling compress the effect (arXiv:2405.06682).

- `9c53a0de2e7964bf` [general] 'Are LLMs Ready for TOON?' (arXiv:2601.12014): the format-without-native-support cost is capacity-dependent. TOON cuts tokens −26.4% vs JSON, −49.4% vs XML, −15.3% vs YAML and estimated carbon −82.9%/−87.6%/−76.9%, but structural correctness collapses — GCS 0.513 vs JSON 0.840, YAML 0.872, XML 0.563 — because TOON is enforced only via prompts. Llama 3.3 70B shows no statistically significant difference (p>0.05 vs all formats), while Gemma 3 4B fails nearly completely (TOON GCS 0.045 vs JSON 0.779) and Qwen 3 4B drops 0.769→0.334. Novel formats are only usable on models with enough capacity to internalize unseen structural constraints.

- `b06e866e7dd44247` [general] Self-Debugging (Chen et al., arXiv:2304.05128, abstract-level; full text unverifiable): generate→execute→feedback→repair is effective when feedback is a deterministic execution signal (rubber-duck debugging: model inspects own execution results and explains its code). Per the paper's abstract: on Spider +2-3% overall and +9% on the hardest problem level; on TransCoder and MBPP (unit tests available) up to +12%; reusing failed predictions with feedback can match or outperform baselines that generate more than 10x candidate programs (~10x sample-efficiency). It matches the condition separating productive from counterproductive repair: a reliable external verifier (execution/tests, oracle labels, stronger or human feedback).

- `b66006c1f5cda644` [general] Tyen et al. (2024, ACL Findings): LLMs cannot reliably find their own reasoning errors (even in objective, unambiguous cases), but can correct them when given the error location; feeding ground-truth mistake-location boosts performance across 5 reasoning tasks. A small classifier trained on out-of-domain data beat prompting a large model at mistake-finding — the bottleneck is error detection, and external/structured detection signals make correction reliable (arXiv:2311.08516).
  links: 1052dd5a927ed18a, ab49a330f50c49ef, f3583d648963c6ab, 888b4004f537880f, 8fe5600483b3642b, a5b5c576f4ffd0b5, 4d082f928948911f, c42c3195c61002fe, 93d3f6c1ebd2262f, 635c8cbfd002fa86, 22abb140e3c15e39, f8edba98b002ec5d, 4cf12f9a8fbae069, 84c8cabac4c26d5f, f103d9ef613d250f, 293c648eb7e6dfe0, fd14a275027254d8
- `fe15f8a0ae73fa1f` [general] Schema Reinforcement Learning (SRL; Lu et al., arXiv:2502.18878) is the training-based 'repair' that moves the validator into training via RL with a fine-grained schema-validator reward, improving native valid-JSON generation without decoding-time constraints: Llama-3.1-8B 36.45% → 79.67% (SRL) vs 60.59% (SFT); Llama-3.2-3B 28.51% → 72.50% (SRL) vs 56.48% (SFT); paper claims up to 16% improvement in valid complex JSON generation vs supervised baselines. Generalizes to tool calling: BFCL-Live overall for Llama-3.1-8B 24.08% → 70.10% (vs 52.69% SFT). Ablation (Llama-3.2-3B complex subset): outcome-reward RL 28.51→31.15%; +Thought-of-Structure reasoning →44.89%; +fine-grained schema validator →35.59%. It is the only measured route that improves the native rate (what retries resample from). Trade-off noted by authors: SFT without collected JSON data degrades reasoning, whereas SRL preserves it.

- `0f112bef6165821c` [repair] Constraint tax / SLM enforcement (measured): hard schema on sub-3B models raises validity 61.5→100% but lowers answer accuracy 19.7→11.0% and raises wrong-valid-schema outputs 49.5→88.9%; a deterministic calendar tool-call loses 43.5 pp executable accuracy at 100% schema validity (102/104 failures = one wrong field); a 3B model still loses 15.3 pp — 'wrong-valid-schema rate' is the first-class reliability metric that counts failures passing parsers, which retries do not fix.

- `b4ac4344ace6c5a2` [general] 'Refining Over Resampling' (arXiv:2608.05643): sampling diversity saturates — on AIME-24 unique semantic reasoning clusters grow only from 1.2 (N=2) to 2.5 (N=32), so additional rollouts repeat dominant patterns. Breadth–depth refinement (N=8 rollouts, D=4 rounds of generator→self-critique→self-correction, then majority vote; verifier-free) consistently beats greedy, Maj@N, verifier-based best-of-N, beam search, and lookahead: Qwen2.5-1.5B MATH500 29.6 (RM@8) → 58.0%; average accuracy 13.21→25.67 (η=17.1 compute-normalized gain over Maj@8). The explicit self-critic stage is necessary and refinement preserves breadth through depth 4. This is the measured mechanism for why retry-by-resampling is exhausted at k≈4–8 while refinement keeps adding; the deterministic-verifier-independent majority-vote aggregation is the external stabilizer.
  links: af2b1630bfe751ef, 8fe5600483b3642b, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, 45e4bccf846fa0ba, 162dbf907c0f473e, a4d77e88c7e61873, dc2abae1015a10fc, b023dfa34d7b6603, 93d3f6c1ebd2262f, 22abb140e3c15e39, 2cafe6061a29c299, 4cf12f9a8fbae069, f103d9ef613d250f, bea9df1d1fc5af19, 293c648eb7e6dfe0, d7e7994431d530e1
- `39c9cdfc179c58ba` [general] State compression in two-agent relays (arXiv:2607.18265): schema-constrained JSON extraction achieves the highest feasibility accuracy 0.96 [0.90,1.00] and eliminates false-NONE (0/50), outperforming the uncompressed control (0.88, 3 false-NONE); a 250-word narrative summary collapses feasibility to 0.48 [0.34,0.62] with 8/50 false-NONEs despite the smallest payload (semantic goal-alignment 0.676 does not preserve numeric/categorical facts); embedding-based pruning matches control. The serialization format of inter-agent state is a measured reliability lever — structured JSON preserves constraint-critical evidence that brevity-oriented compression destroys.

- `73c291787a0887ce` [general] JSON Schema Test Suite coverage by engine (of 45 feature categories): Guidance full coverage in 13, moderate in 21; Llama.cpp 1/5; XGrammar 1/3; Outlines 0/2. Compile errors: Outlines 42, Llama.cpp 37, Guidance 25, XGrammar 3. Over-constrained: Llama.cpp 18, Outlines 16, Guidance 7, XGrammar 5. Under-constrained: XGrammar 38, Outlines 8, Llama.cpp 7, Guidance 1. Over-constraining suppresses valid outputs; under-constraining is the direct cause of residual failures that force retries.

- `8ab263a3dc129e33` [general] Five established mechanisms avoid full recomputation, all from primary docs: (1) delta-capture + replay via change logs/triggers (Oracle fast refresh + MV logs, pg_ivm AFTER triggers); (2) index-structured materialization updated per changed document (CouchDB B-tree views); (3) view-as-physical-index maintained by DML (SQL Server indexed views); (4) batch incremental pipelines with high-watermark + upsert keys (dbt); (5) continuous/streaming maintenance with durable or in-memory storage (Materialize, BigQuery incremental MVs).
  links: cfddc08e47e6dcf7, 266b8325088b1614, 3faec3e0b1d80803, 9b9296bb8b2fc470, 8c38fd0e61beee75, 3c52730566932ab6, ed80831515a47d10, ae4ee7c4c7369b1f, 5d32c1280761f55f, 6dbd271b7ce0e25e, 1a44ed072b912b06, 16f2c921fa480440, e761f79662ed051f, 00d9b00db4beee22
- `0503a43026e3c7bc` [general] The multiplicative repetition penalty shipped across HuggingFace, vLLM, llama.cpp and a dozen engines corrupts schema compliance because its sign-branch reads a gauge-dependent quantity (the model's arbitrary logit zero-point) (Hollows, arXiv:2607.09791; abstract-level). At θ=1.3 it changes 58–96% of greedy tokens and drops the rate of valid schema-conformant output from 97% to 23% on 200 real JSON schemas (five ≤7B models). Fix: apply the penalty to normalized log-probabilities instead of raw logits (HuggingFace ships LogitNormalization, off by default). Structured-output compliance measurements must pin the full serving stack (sampler settings, zero-point gauge), not only the model/grammar.
  links: 104c7db4c0a0fa2a, 3137514f89e89347, e88eb9f15941f838, 2df44a2e25263275, 4cb71d0255cccf49, 45e4bccf846fa0ba, 4d082f928948911f, 9c53a0de2e7964bf, 22afd8a8bede9bbe, 308c11e281ffbca4, 90b87f746aa7223d, 06cc8dfc000757fa, 01f00e1e3835a627
- `0f3c6731224b1b90` [general] IFEval-FC (arXiv:2509.18420): format constraints embedded in a JSON schema's description field are not enforced by constrained decoding, and models fail them at high rates. No evaluated model surpassed 80% accuracy on 750 function-calling test cases; e.g., Claude Opus 4.1 — Letter Frequency 28%, N All Capital Words 20%, Spaces In Between 8%; GPT-5 minimal — Letter Frequency 36%. Trivially-easy constraints hitting 90–100% were deliberately excluded. Human-readable format rules in descriptions remain a prompt-following problem, not an enforcement problem.

- `3137514f89e89347` [general] SchemaBench (Lu/Li/Cong, arXiv:2502.18878) measured native schema-valid JSON-string generation (schema-only, greedy, ~40K schemas, avg nesting depth 16.7): overall correctness GPT-4o 61.06%, GPT-4o-mini 43.98%, Qwen-2.5-7B 42.38%, Llama-3.1-8B 36.45%, Llama-3.2-3B 28.51%, MiniCPM-3-4B 27.77%. GPT-4o per-subtask: Complex-schema 84.47%, Custom-format 61.56%, Escape-translation 37.14% (open models 8–16%). Failure decomposition: GPT-4o 13% validation + 8% parser errors; Llama-3.2-3B 23%, MiniCPM 36% parser errors; ~5% floor of pattern-regex errors. Together the two benchmarks bracket the no-enforcement failure regime at roughly 40–90% failure on complex schemas.
  links: e2c28a05b7ee21b1, 4cb71d0255cccf49, 473e91ddc0c06971, 1d482a9b4839f28a, 45e4bccf846fa0ba, 4d082f928948911f, 9c53a0de2e7964bf, 0503a43026e3c7bc, a4d77e88c7e61873, fe15f8a0ae73fa1f, 9edd522ac96ebcbc, f4090a7c63c389b2, 0a6c3cc372c5cf77
- `68944cdb3db0a2fc` [general] Retrieval techniques are complements, not alternatives — the biggest gains come from stacking them: Zep = temporal KG + hybrid lexical/dense/graph retrieval; HippoRAG = KG + embeddings + PPR; LongMemEval's best config = session decomposition + fact-augmented keys + time-aware query expansion. Sources: arXiv:2501.13956; arXiv:2405.14831; arXiv:2410.10813v2.
  links: b6fef0270ed8d28d, 59735e32b612df1a, 50662f026de1acfe, 2c6fb8ddf6a41794, e18bd137f36455e4, 999b1d884e08eb37, 832a7c538944c10a, dca21576c4c8bae6, cf94cceed826d20c, 348e2784f9115e96, e286837b45d310e8, 15116661844082ce, 0369c5606fbf5ccc
- `8baa3b2f82d7faa6` [general] Opinion (sourced inference, not a measured study): the selector for agents is which failure you must survive and at what cost — WAL+checkpoint gives strongest crash consistency and minimal replay but needs a storage-engine contract and is overkill for small agent state; snapshot-restore is simplest with a bounded recovery window but loses everything since the last snapshot unless combined with a log (RPO = snapshot interval); event sourcing enables exact resume, audit, time travel, and human-in-the-loop review at the cost of side-effect duplication, unbounded history requiring compaction/pruning, and schema/version evolution of events.
  links: 5ff5c00c83663882, 5f26c9330e251481, 304e41006f05da56, 185255b5f360bea5, eb55e129103e8a6f, ecca3611145516e6, a40757229890299e, b556d77f45006ee2, 7fcceed280fcc7b0, a495d4c500ec01b5, 6665ad2250b0bf6a, 12fe0bcb03d20370, dcff2b5ab8c2f74e
- `a3d101849aff988d` [general] Instruction Stacking Collapse (arXiv:2608.02639): follow rate falls from ~96% at one instruction to 0.60 (Sonnet), 0.43 (Gemini), 0.20 (GPT-5-mini) at twenty — sigmoid, non-linear; 'a single output JSON constraint is jointly unsatisfiable with nine others' — of 231 instruction pairs, 15 are logical impossibilities, and ~12% of satisfiable pairs fail far more than their parts predict (the JSON × title-length pair is −1.00); failure is structured interference, reproducible across models, not uniform forgetting. A training-free instruction compiler gives capability-graded recovery: +11.0 pp (GPT-5-mini), +3.3 pp (Gemini), −1.2 pp (Sonnet). Stacking a JSON-output requirement with other constraints is harmful even at the prompt level.
  links: cfa462ba820abdc5, 2df44a2e25263275, a4951002397ee060, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, 308c11e281ffbca4, 8eb9a877c6a47673, f15a4d58c0bb1a4a, 1ab99428a950f6a4, 86be403af727cc0f, 15b82401938c5a93, 1f894d76e5789412, d84e1b455ff7763a, f26ea5ec230f7ffb
- `bcac82d5414851c8` [repair] Benchmark evaluator mechanisms (Vaghasiya et al.): deterministic evaluators fail by brittle state matching, trajectory lock-in, incorrect ground truths, substring-based assertions, and reward-basis misalignment — in the inspected BFCL v4 multi-turn export, 80% of official failures are instance_state_mismatch, which 'conflates genuine task failures with evaluator artifacts'; a τ²-bench false negative rejected a correct $708 answer for lacking the substring '1628' ('an incorrect semantic target implemented as a substring check'); a false positive showed inaction passing because unchanged DB state looks successful.

- `c1a0b768075c2467` [general] Verdict of the research: besides MAST there is a substantial, actively growing body of empirically derived LLM-agent failure taxonomies, all reporting explicit measurement corpora (trace counts, annotator counts, agreement statistics, task/turn counts); the strongest-documented are the Interaction-Centric Taxonomy (41 modes; 40 grounded worked examples; judge reproducibility kappa=0.76), DEFT (14 modes; grounded theory over ~1,000 DRA reports with Krippendorff's-alpha validation), Aegis (6 modes; 142 annotated failed traces), DeepVerifier (5 classes/13 sub-classes; 555 annotated error points), and the longitudinal silent-failure taxonomy (5 classes; 22 production incidents).
  links: 973e990075c8a7fd, aa17692c22a78738, f7a62764ca74d0e9, 38956b8103d9e760, f4b23532d8f51878, 984129939642011a, 57c6cc3485ade4ed, bacb0e234ea35e04, b023dfa34d7b6603, 7486a7f9f63098ee, f8edba98b002ec5d, cb3c088728b3c87c, 57d4732c1ffb43bf
- `db298e711a8209ed` [general] AgentTether (Zhao et al., Microsoft Research, arXiv:2607.06273, verified in full text): graph-localized diagnosis + behavior-scoped guidance + repair memory beats other repair patterns for failed multi-step tool-use runs. Measured on 261 τ-bench tasks (Qwen3.7-max), ≤3-iteration repair budget: overall (n=123) Blind-retry 43.09% → Outcome-feedback 39.02% (worse than blind retry) → Reflexion 44.72% (+1.63) → AgentTether-post-run 60.16% (+17.07) → AgentTether 69.11% (+26.02); on hardest Banking domain (n=83) blind 26.51% → AgentTether 59.04% (+32.53 pp); guarded run-time intervention adds +12.05 pp over post-run-only. Root cause precedes symptom by a median of 4 required steps (up to 26), upstream in 79% of runs; one root error cascades into 3.2 further violated checks; one-shot diagnosis adherence decays below 50% by step 13 (step 8 on Banking) — diagnosis must be carried and re-applied at run-time. AgentTether reduces agent turns (66.5→56.4) and end-to-end tokens (1,376K→1,197K) — repair is cheaper than re-executing. Outcome feedback alone can be counterproductive in policy-constrained domains.
  links: 5e22ecc2aa426efa, 229361857af8028c, 1052dd5a927ed18a, a4d77e88c7e61873, 6bf04b5388b27261, 605232ab8cd262a4, 90b87f746aa7223d, b06e866e7dd44247, 1ab99428a950f6a4, 22abb140e3c15e39, f8edba98b002ec5d, b8b7f1d1340fadc7, 1f6daa3b273092cd
- `dc2abae1015a10fc` [general] Test-time-compute economics of retries: (1) Self-Consistency (arXiv:2203.11171) — sampling N paths + majority vote beats sample-and-rank, beam search, and prompt-ensembles at the same sample count, and answer consistency correlates with accuracy (a cheap know-when-it-doesn't-know signal usable to stop or escalate a retry loop). (2) Scaling LLM Test-Time Compute (arXiv:2408.03314) — compute-optimal difficulty-binned allocation 'can nearly outperform best-of-N using up to 4x less test-time compute (16 vs 64 generations)'; verifier search over-optimizes on easy questions (beam search degrades as budget grows), best-of-N better at high budgets/easy questions, search better at low budgets/hard; model-predicted difficulty nearly matches oracle. Uniform retries are up to 4x more expensive than difficulty-adaptive budgets.
  links: 3c233462b12abfbd, 2df44a2e25263275, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, b4ac4344ace6c5a2, b023dfa34d7b6603, cee7417852b29375, 635c8cbfd002fa86, 1150dfe53f759651, b06e866e7dd44247, f103d9ef613d250f, 15b82401938c5a93, da2c3504efdd2f5e, d7e7994431d530e1
- `f15a4d58c0bb1a4a` [general] Natural Language Tools (NLT) replication (arXiv:2607.03953; 14 models, 8,560 trials): free-form natural-language tool calls beat structured tool calling — overall accuracy 62.3% vs 47.4% (+14.9 pp), 93% fewer critical errors (51 vs 755), 25.2% lower token usage. Claude-Sonnet-4 +43.1 pp, Mistral-7B +39.4 pp (0.0 vs 39.4 — complete structured collapse, 320 errors). The advantage reverses only for structured-optimized frontier models (Gemini-2.5-Pro −33.7 pp, GPT-OSS-120B −6.4 pp). Survivorship-bias correction (any condition with ≥70/80 errors → 0%) is methodologically important: raw structured accuracy 51.9% is inflated by near-total-failure conditions. 'A structured failure triggers retries, fallback routing, and coordination overhead, while NLT avoids most of that cost at the source.'

- `f3b751d3cfdaee27` [repair] TCMIIES (system report): field-level extraction accuracy plateaued at 81.6% (Research Topic 89.3% down to Research Limitations 73.5%) despite prompt+retry — the schema-valid-but-wrong gap; first-attempt success 88.4% rose to 97.1% after the three-retry mechanism, with 100% completion under 10% injected API failures.

- `0369c5606fbf5ccc` [general] MERIT (Vo et al., MBZUAI/HCMUT, arXiv:2608.05906, verified in full text): cross-query repair memory (retaining oracle-verified corrections as positive memory and unsuccessful directions as negative, with a deterministic error-type classifier and hybrid dense+BM25 retriever) improves repair over stateless iterative repair on Text-to-SQL: Spider 66.34→69.79 (paired Δ+3.45 pp, 95% CI [+1.97,+4.96]); BIRD 47.35→48.44 (Δ+1.09 pp, weaker evidence). But MERIT is NOT reliably better than untyped dynamic retrieval (tie on Spider, +0.28 pp on BIRD) — the added structure is worth ≈0; Reflexion-style verbal memory beats MERIT on BIRD (51.24 vs 48.44, Δ−2.80, CI [−4.15,−1.46]) at ~2.4x token cost. Negative memory contributes little (−0.13/−0.09 pp); schema-local experience is the most consistent benefit (cross-database-only retrieval costs −0.93 pp Spider, −2.74 pp BIRD). Memory does not reduce total tokens vs stateless (Spider 5.27M vs 2.53M, BIRD 17.6M vs 9.3M).

- `18005e6da326e5fc` [general] Constrained decoding can be faster than unconstrained: Guidance 6.37 ms/output-token vs LM-only 15.40 ms on GlaiveAI (the paper's ~50% speedup headline); Llama.cpp 29.98 ms; Outlines 30.33 ms with 3.48 s one-time grammar-compilation cost. Enforcement is not inherently slower, but implementation matters.

- `293c648eb7e6dfe0` [repair] Intrinsic self-correction (measured, repair): without an external signal consistently lowers accuracy — GSM8K Llama-2 62.0→36.5, GPT-4 95.5→89.0 (1→5 calls, up to −25 pts) while multiplying call cost 1→5×; repair only reliably helps with external feedback — oracle labels, execution/unit-test signals (Self-Debugging +2–9% to +12%), or stronger/human feedback (1.58× more repaired programs pass, 33.3%→52.6%); at equal budget repair often ties or loses to plain i.i.d. resampling.

- `74a333f06b0e1b1b` [general] Recurring across independent LLM-routing papers: (1) the achievable tradeoff is dominated by the size of the weak/strong quality gap and the training-data distribution match (augmentation and low-data regimes matter more than router architecture); (2) routers over-deploy the strong model in practice (RouterArena normalized deferral curve), so the frontier is far from the oracle; (3) router overhead itself is measurable and non-trivial (latency, embedding API calls, per-request cost) but small vs generation cost.

- `8df10858c5595438` [repair] Semantic ceiling at 100% schema validity (measured): SOB best leaf-value accuracy 83.0% text / 67.2% image / 23.7% audio; OrderBench 100% schema-valid with 2–83% semantic across models; the gap compounds with schema breadth and context length — xmemory single-pass frontier output-level exactness collapses to 34–62% even at 95–97% field-level F1; ExtractBench 0% valid output on a 369-field schema (4.6% field-level pass); ScrapeGraphAI-100k (93,695 real events): validity 98→20% at depth ≥7, ~65% at ≥200 keys, ~55% at complexity>1000 (87.4% GPT-4o-mini).

- `8fe5600483b3642b` [general] Established synthesis: the failure point in self-correction is error detection, not correction — Tyen et al. (location signals restore correction), Self-Refine's '94% everything looks good,' and the detection/miscorrection-decomposition preprint converge here; designs that externalize the critic (verifier model, tools, classifiers, verify-first prompting, RL-trained self-verification) improve reliability, while designs asking the generator to judge its own ungrounded reasoning add little or hurt.

- `b25f3d9404e1c402` [repair] Error-budget masking mechanism (Jang et al.): the score stays flat because the benchmark allows ten failed tool calls per episode and per-error recovery is equally reliable at both precisions (55% recovery rate unchanged) — 'the loop actively repairs the errors quantization introduces before they reach the outcome' at the cost of 2.5× more failed calls per episode (1.45→3.64) and 29% more tool calls (7.3→9.4); falsifiable proof: shrinking the error budget K re-exposes the damage — the BF16−INT4 gap grows 1.3 pp (K=10) to 7.5 (K=5) to 16.7 pp (K=2), ~13× wider, only in the volume-adding cell; budget-exhausting terminations rise 9× (5→46 of 456); mechanism: logit-margin account — full-precision failure propensity gates susceptibility (near-zero-baseline Qwen cells not amplified; checkpoint identity, not size/architecture, decides).

- `bea9df1d1fc5af19` [repair] IBM CW (system report): the decisive structural-vs-semantic gap (independent Claude Sonnet 4.5 judge at temperature 0, majority-vote over 3 gradings × 1,044 total) — even the strongest model, llama-4-maverick on v2, satisfied only 55.2% of prompts (mean 71.5), next-best 44.8%; 'no benchmarked model achieved reliable correctness' and 'human review of generated workflows before execution remains necessary'; structural validity and semantic correctness are nearly orthogonal at production scale.

- `ef6a82f7cb4bf553` [general] XGrammar (arXiv:2411.15100, MLSys '25): CFG mask-generation cost can go below the LLM's own per-token latency — under 40 µs/token for JSON Schema and unconstrained JSON (Llama-3.1-8B, RTX 4090), up to 3x speedup on JSON Schema and 100x on CFG vs baselines, up to 80x end-to-end on H100; near-zero overhead with CPU/GPU overlap. Context-dependent tokens <1% of vocab (1,134/128k), reduced to 120 by context expansion; mask cache drops 160 MB → 0.46 MB. Caveat: XGrammar achieves this at the cost of under-constraining (highest under-constrained failure count, 38 categories).

- `0a3013221a215aa2` [general] No single primary study directly A/Bs selective retrieval vs summary vs compression vs full-context and reports token savings as the outcome metric for long-running agents was found; each source reports a different metric (accuracy at fixed context, context-window multiplier, compression ratio, per-call context bounds). Settling evidence would be a controlled experiment on a long-running agent benchmark (e.g., SWE-bench agentic runs or long-horizon tool use) measuring billed input tokens and task success across the four arms with identical model and task; to the reviewer's knowledge no such published benchmark exists.

On DMR (500 multi-session conversations): Zep 94.8% vs MemGPT 93.4% vs full-context 94.4% vs session summaries 78.6%. Caveat: Zep is vendor-authored and the DMR gap to full-context is small (0.4). Source: arXiv:2501.13956, Tables 1-3.

- `7014b9ee9ab22a85` [repair] Cross-pattern synthesis (§4–§6): when the verifier is deterministic (schema validator / SQL execution / exact check), a retry+validation loop reliably lifts structured-output success to ~90–97% and never regresses; the residual failure is not syntax or schema but semantic leaf values, which deterministic validation cannot catch — measured plateau ~63% (leaf) to ~80% (transaction semantics).

- `9cd6a6c67868558e` [general] LongMemEval: 500 manually curated questions over task-oriented user-assistant histories evaluating five abilities — information extraction (IE), multi-session reasoning (MR), temporal reasoning (TR), knowledge updates (KU), and abstention (ABS); seven question types; two standard settings: LongMemEval_S (~115k tokens/question) and LongMemEval_M (500 sessions, ~1.5M tokens).

- `add1799958c47c93` [general] 'The Tell-Tale Trace' (Sourav & Balwani, arXiv:2608.03291, verified in full text): generic retry reproduces the failure mode while procedure-directed repair rescues it, measured on solver-certified SAT/UNSAT CNF reasoning. SAT failures show 'premature verification collapse' (wrong traces half as long, more verification-heavy and cyclic, lower-entropy, finalizing earlier 77.1% vs 89.8%, clause coverage unchanged). UNSAT failures substitute assignment search for contradiction proof. Early-warning is model-dependent (80.2% of Llama3-8B wrong traces flagged early, only 35.7% for Llama3-70B). The decisive repair result (Table 2, Llama3-70B, 52 wrong-SAT completions): generic retry ('try again carefully', no strategy) → 10.0% accuracy (correcting 6/52, 11.5%); targeted proof-search prompt → 85.0% accuracy (correcting 44/52, 84.6%) — paired +73.1 pp, McNemar-significant. This is the controlled measurement that 'retry harder' and 'retry smarter' are different mechanisms — the identical model on identical instances goes from 11.5% to 84.6% corrected solely by replacing a strategy-free retry with a procedure-directed one. Caveats: post-hoc and oracle-assisted, generic-retry baseline unmatched in prompt length, and early-warning 'is not a general failure detector'.
  links: 554b0217976296b3, 1d482a9b4839f28a, a4951002397ee060, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, edfc9ea838f66df0, 308c11e281ffbca4, 7263566e1ca0c610, 93d3f6c1ebd2262f, 84c8cabac4c26d5f, 15b82401938c5a93, ef69e42494b930cc
- `c81957cf3aac4c06` [general] Confused Deputy (OAuth proxy) attack: a proxy with static client ID + dynamic client registration + consent cookies lets an attacker steal an authorization code; mitigations MUST include per-client server-side consent, exact-string redirect_uri validation, cryptographically random single-use state set only after consent, __Host- prefixed Secure/HttpOnly/SameSite=Lax consent cookies, and frame-ancestors CSP / X-Frame-Options DENY (Security Best Practices).
  links: ddf4bfc7760221e4, af0d9ca54cec9c18, 57ed22f30f0c6760, 9eba1c8e9d2d65b7, c3484bbb30701da2, e4344628bee35e4c, 0d9ab0996bcbca80, 9a9c35587bb37962, 26a851d4bc8775f8, 3286897269792642, 6d2f6996dd28f2d0
- `1f6daa3b273092cd` [repair] Diagnosis-guided runtime repair (measured): AgentTether repairs 59–65% of failed τ-bench runs on the hardest domain, beating blind retry, plain outcome feedback, and self-reflection at lower turn/token cost; feedback content matters — VeriHarness: adding location+value+admissible alternatives to repair feedback gains +42/+44 pp, and JSON-vs-prose presentation is neutral ('no evidence that JSON syntax itself improves repair').

- `266b8325088b1614` [general] pg_ivm (PostgreSQL extension) computes and applies only incremental changes to materialized views, updated immediately in AFTER triggers when a base table is modified, instead of recomputing from scratch as REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW does.

- `2c6fb8ddf6a41794` [general] Time-aware memory retrieval measurably improves temporal-reasoning recall: LongMemEval time-aware query expansion improves recall by an average of 11.3% using rounds as the value and 6.8% using sessions (6.8%-11.3% 'when a strong LLM is employed for query expansion'). Source: arXiv:2410.10813v2, Section 5.4/Table 4 and abstract.

- `4b26f96529333302` [repair] BFCL live numbers here are the 2025-12-16 archive snapshot (evaluation commit f7cf735); the leaderboard's later 2026-04-12 update has a JS-rendered unextractable table so the two could differ slightly; an independent expert audit puts the evaluator's own error rate at ~20% for BFCL v4 (state-mismatch artifacts dominate) and 30.5% for LLM-judged LiveMCPBench — all function-calling leaderboard numbers should be read as ranking-level, not point-accurate; rate-limit-specific dollar-per-retry costs for commercial APIs were not located in primary literature (TCMIIES $0.38–3.41 per 1,000 papers and BFCL full-eval $1–355 per model both include non-retry spend).
