- `605232ab8cd262a4` [general] BFCL V4 live numbers (BFCL-Result archive, 2025-12-16): top model Claude-Opus-4-5 (FC) 77.47% overall; even the best current model fails ~23% of function-calling tasks. Multi-turn is hardest: best Opus-4-5 68.38%, GPT-5.2 28.12%, o4-mini 16.62%, most open models 0–50%. Irrelevance detection (correctly refusing to call) is a persistent weak spot (several strong models <40%). Format-sensitivity of prompt-mode models ranges from 8.5 (Gemini-3-Pro) to 81.5 (Phi-4) — prompt-based function calling can lose up to ~80 points when the output format shifts. Full-eval cost spans $1–355 across models.

- `6977145a51d83491` [general] Hybrid LLM routing value depends sharply on the model-pair quality gap: at a 20% cost advantage the small-gap pair (Llama-2 7b/13b) loses <=0.1% BART quality, the large-gap pair (FLAN-t5 800m/Llama-2 13b) loses up to 5.8%. When the gap is large, training on Pr[H>=0] labels is near-useless ('0 for almost 90% of queries'); a label-relaxation transformation (Pr[H>=−t]) recovers a usable signal. (Hybrid LLM, arXiv 2404.14618v1, Table 1, §3.3, Fig 4a)
  links: 53b44257c13294e1, cef70c90b6220520, e151279bfa369f9a, 135c69977d75322e, feeed923077d1e1f, 74a333f06b0e1b1b, 6eb2a6b0a6c49c71, 4d082f928948911f, f103d9ef613d250f, 293c648eb7e6dfe0
- `6e1f98afd067dae7` [repair] Palabrita resolution architecture (reported): defensive parsing as a pipeline (UTF-8 sanitize → code-fence strip → direct decode → regex extract → structural type-based field inference), contextual retry feeding the specific rejection reason back into the prompt (word-length errors the main trigger), and 'progressive simplification' — the LLM's job reduced from 7 output fields to 0 for word generation (curated word lists) and 2 fields for hints, with a deterministic fallback so 'the LLM enhances the experience; it does not gate it'.
  links: cfa462ba820abdc5, 4d082f928948911f, 61d5bd996f9f2ad9, 7486a7f9f63098ee, a090ad17666f0913, 5480bd685bff8004, 15b82401938c5a93, 70c12cac8fc7ae06, f0afcf1604a978a8, d84e1b455ff7763a
- `9266ab4c52fd8d11` [general] Token format of constrained output is a free reliability lever ('Lost in Space', arXiv:2502.14969; 1.28M GCD classifications, 4 families × 8 sizes, llama.cpp GBNF): real numbers in [0,1] perform best across all models (Spearman ρ 0.420–0.609) while integers/binaries are worst (integer ρ −0.160 for Llama 3.1 8b); leading-whitespace tokens improve performance 5–10% because LW and non-LW token twins are not exchangeable in embedding space (cosine 0.369–0.386 vs ~0.008–0.016 random) and LW tokens were 2.5–2.7x more prevalent in pre-training data; word-form outputs consistently worse than digits (ρ −0.12 to −0.41). Format sensitivity strongest for smaller models. A grammar admitting semantically-equivalent but differently-tokenized output can cost ~0.5 Spearman correlation.

- `ae4ee7c4c7369b1f` [general] BigQuery has two materialized-view classes: incremental views, which BigQuery precomputes/updates in the background from base-table changes, and non-incremental views, where every refresh runs the full query; freshness is served by reading rest of the data from the MV and only the changes from base tables.

- `c0b3c5493790ad8f` [general] NeuroVLM-Bench (arXiv:2603.24846): 20 frontier MLLMs, 29,538 neuroimaging samples, 6-field strict JSON schema. Valid-JSON was generally high but Grok 4 and Claude Sonnet 4.5 exhibited significantly lower valid-JSON rates (84–87%); MedGemma-1.5-4B, MedGemma-1-4B, LLaMA-4-Maverick, Qwen2.5-VL-32B maintained perfect validity with near-zero abstention; LLaMA-3.2-Vision (11B/90B) failed almost entirely via excessive abstention. Models differ far less in basic metadata recognition than in clinically meaningful diagnostic reasoning — the schema-valid≠correct pattern reproduces in the multimodal domain.

- `d897024c3f240a4b` [general] DEFT deep-research failure taxonomy (Zhang et al., OPPO AI Agent Team; arXiv 2512.01948) defines 14 failure modes under 3 core categories: Reasoning (Failure to Understand Requirements 10.55%, Lack of Analytical Depth 11.09%, Limited Analytical Scope 0.90%, Rigid Planning Strategy 5.60%), Retrieval (Insufficient External Information Acquisition 16.30%, Information Handling Deficiency 2.26%, Information Integration Failure 2.91%, Information Representation Misalignment 2.91%, Verification Mechanism Failure 8.72%), Generation (Redundant Content Piling 2.51%, Structural Organization Dysfunction 2.26%, Content Specification Deviation 10.73%, Deficient Analytical Rigor 4.31%, Strategic Content Fabrication 18.95%).
  links: e368dbd927efdfc0, 8479b0749854fe46, aa17692c22a78738, 38956b8103d9e760, f4b23532d8f51878, 02a02d256e8ffada, 7e96686c5bcb8461, 89d1caf3454ccaea, 4d082f928948911f, 84c8cabac4c26d5f
- `e286837b45d310e8` [general] Static-dynamic dilemma (RoMem primary text): 'most existing approaches model time as discrete metadata, either sorting by recency (burying old-yet-permanent knowledge), simply overwriting outdated facts, or requiring an expensive LLM call at every ingestion step, leaving them unable to distinguish persistent facts from evolving ones.' Failure directions: recency sorting silently buries static knowledge (decades-old fact ranked below fresher irrelevant entries); disabling recency bias leaves temporal conflicts unresolved, confusing the downstream LLM. RoMem asserts its append-only store works only because contradictions are resolved geometrically at query time, and claims all existing baselines (Mem0, Zep, LicoMemory, HippoRAG) treat memory as a static snapshot, causing contradictory facts to cluster together in retrieval space and confuse the LLM.
  links: ccecea1af23feed5, 68944cdb3db0a2fc, 70d2e0f66bef8b38, a6e2c69032154967, 348e2784f9115e96, 9fd23bfe4eb2af7f, 3b3bae3fd783ee40, 6665ad2250b0bf6a, 4ca81b227fadc600, 0369c5606fbf5ccc
- `e2c28a05b7ee21b1` [general] PSC / Parser Stack Classification (arXiv:2608.03065, ISSTA 2026) makes GCD mask computation vocabulary-independent: the set of parser stacks accepting a token is a regular language, so a single precompiled FSA classifies the parser stack once per step (O(1) mask lookup). Measured vs XGrammar/GreatGramma/Formatron/LLGuidance: mask computation up to 700x faster on complex PL grammars (Java/Go/SQL), up to 30x on schema-conformant JSON, end-to-end throughput approaching unconstrained decoding with correctness on par (99.6–100% processed, no parser errors). Preprocessing cost is small for simple grammars, significant for complex ones; results shareable across users.

- `f350dbda4620ffdd` [general] Detail-level extraction: fact-extraction compression trades recall of detail — 'While further compression into individual user facts harms overall performance due to information loss, it improves the multi-session reasoning accuracy.' LoCoMo likewise: session-summary RAG does not improve QA, likely due to loss of information during conversion of dialogs to summaries.

- `f4596f48ade2d79f` [repair] Deployment structure dominates model choice (measured): decomposing complex workflow-JSON generation into piecewise stages raises structural success 31.5–82.8% → 74.1–97.8% across six models (4/6 above 90%, zero before); minimal-diff repair (RFC 6902 patches, JSON Whisperer) matches full-regeneration quality within 5% at 31% fewer tokens and 42.3% lower latency/cost (failure modes: missed related updates + array-index arithmetic) — third independent confirmation of selective-repair economics after StepCache/Asthana.

- `01a2672e8349e37a` [repair] Training-side format cliff (measured): on-policy distillation reward-extrapolation collapses the JSON contract above a closed-form threshold — parse 94.8→65.1→8.5% across λ=1.15→1.50 while ranking quality on parsed outputs stays flat; below-λ a 1.7B matches 8B-SFT at 1/5 the parameters; constrained decoding adds +0.009.

- `1052dd5a927ed18a` [general] LoCoMo has three tasks: (1) QA in five categories — single-hop, multi-hop, temporal reasoning, open-domain (commonsense/world) knowledge, and adversarial (designed to trick the agent into providing wrong answers, with expectation the agent identifies them as unanswerable); (2) event-graph summarization scored with FactScore against the ground-truth temporal event graph; (3) multi-modal dialogue generation scored with MMRelevance.

- `1150dfe53f759651` [general] 'Is Self-Repair a Silver Bullet for Code Generation?' (Olausson et al., ICLR 2024, arXiv:2306.09896): cost-aware repair gains are modest at equal budget and sometimes negative — GPT-3.5 on HumanEval ≤ +3%, Code Llama on HumanEval ≤ +10%, GPT-4 on APPS ≤ +8% over i.i.d. sampling at the same sample budget. Sample-budget allocation matters: on APPS, GPT-4 with 10 diverse initial samples + 1 repair each = 1.05x pass@20, but 2 initial + 10 repairs each = 0.97x vs pass@22 — over-investing in repair loses to plain resampling. Repair is bottlenecked by feedback quality: human feedback vs GPT-4's own raised repaired-program pass rate from 33.3% to 52.6% (1.58x); GPT-4 feedback judged inaccurate in 32/80 cases vs 7/80 for humans.

- `22afd8a8bede9bbe` [general] Tam et al. 'Let Me Speak Freely?' (arXiv:2408.02442) — origin of the format-tax finding: format restrictions degrade LLM reasoning while stricter constraints degrade more. Headline drops (exact-match): GSM8K Claude-3-Haiku 86.5→23.4 under JSON (−63 pts); GPT-3.5-Turbo 75.99→49.25; LLaMA-3-8B 75.13→48.90. Degradation is not parsing failures (Last-Letter JSON parsing error rate 0.148% yet 38.15-point performance gap). Task-dependent: same constraints help classification (Gemini DDXPlus 41.6→60.3) — 'format restriction hurts reasoning, helps classification'.

- `3880083953a8f0d3` [general] Gradle Test Retry plugin retries failed tests within the same task up to maxRetries (default 0 = off); defaults: failOnPassedAfterRetry=false (pass-on-retry does not fail the task), failOnSkippedAfterRetry=true, maxFailures=0 (circuit-breaker cap off). Supports include/exclude filters by class name and annotations, plus whole-class retry (classRetry) for Spock @Stepwise/TestNG dependsOn. The README warns retrying tests alone is not a viable flaky-test mitigation strategy and should accompany tracking/fixing.

- `672b4912c42144b2` [general] DMR measures single-session-hop fact recall (opinion grounded in source): whether an agent can pull one specific fact out of a prior session; it tests retrieval/consistency, not updating, temporal reasoning, or abstention. Zep's primary text criticizes it: 'The evaluation relies exclusively on single-turn, fact-retrieval questions... Each conversation contains only 60 messages, easily fitting within current LLM context windows.'

- `a101c8e7ede3b6b3` [general] Second ExtractBench (Zhang et al., LlamaIndex, arXiv:2607.29677): 4,869 pages / 370 docs / 8 domains, long-document value F1 collapses for commercial VLMs (Gemini 3.5 Flash 87.9% short → 27.9% long; GPT-5.4 Nano 77.4 → 35.8) while coding agents/specialized APIs stay high (LlamaExtract Agentic Plus 96.6/93.3/94.4; Codex GPT-5.5 95.7→78.9). Failure is recall-concentrated (truncation not misreading): precision stays high while recall collapses (Gemini 3.5 Flash Δ+57.2). Enormous tables: specialized/coding systems 78.9–95.9 vs ≤7.2 for every direct VLM. Two end-to-end zero-output patterns: (a) 0.0 on twin-schema case and nothing on largest-schema bundles; (b) all Claude one-shot VLM tiers reject Automotive and K-1 schemas outright (33 rejections, 8.9% of pool, score zero). Model tier alone does not predict quality; best quality–cost point LlamaExtract Agentic Plus 95.6 F1 at 8.1 ¢/page vs Codex GPT-5.5 93.6 at 27.8 ¢/page. Grounding: word-level grounding F1 only specialized APIs return (best 46.4); every commercial VLM/coding agent scores 0.0.

- `a6e2c69032154967` [general] LoCoMo findings: long-context LLMs/RAG improve QA 22–66% but significantly lag human levels (by 56%), especially in temporal reasoning (by 73%); long-context models are especially prone to misassigning dialogs or events to the wrong speaker; gpt-3.5-turbo-16k scored 2.1% on adversarial questions. RoMem later classifies LoCoMo as the 'hybrid' benchmark: a mixture of dynamic temporal updates and general knowledge queries.

- `ca79380b2a16a6e8` [general] Zep vs MemGPT vs full-context on DMR (500 MSC conversations, gpt-4-turbo): MemGPT 93.4%, Zep 94.8%, full-conversation baseline 94.4%, session-summary baseline 78.6%, recursive summarization 35.3%.

- `ccecea1af23feed5` [general] Mem0's automatic extraction is additive by default: on changed facts it stores the new fact without silently rewriting the old one; correction requires explicit update/delete operations; the store separates SQL (facts/metadata as source of truth), vector (embeddings), and entity stores, fusing semantic, keyword, entity, and temporal retrieval signals. Source: Mem0 docs 'How Mem0 Works'.

- `d32f5cbbb9dfef2a` [general] DMR is the task where a static fact store succeeds: it has no update, temporal, or abstention categories, and Zep shows a plain full-conversation baseline already matches MemGPT (94.4% vs 93.4%, both gpt-4-turbo; Zep 94.8%). Hence high DMR scores co-exist with failures on LongMemEval/LoCoMo.

- `e5a6d2ccb1b2bd7a` [repair] Agentic fault prevalence (Shah et al.): 'Data & Validation Errors' is the single most common observable symptom (75 occurrences, 20.0% of all faults), ahead of installation/dependency issues (13.3%) and runtime failures (10.7%); the two dominant root causes are Dependency and Integration Failures (19.5%) and Data and Type Handling Failures (17.6%), interpreted as 'systematic contract violations between probabilistically generated artifacts and the deterministic interface, type, and runtime constraints enforced by external software systems'.
  links: e232f1c2ce57f2cf, 7e96686c5bcb8461, 89d1caf3454ccaea, 93d3f6c1ebd2262f, 22abb140e3c15e39, f8edba98b002ec5d, 4cf12f9a8fbae069, 57d4732c1ffb43bf, f6c08ef82bdb0411
- `e5e61f2516f62242` [general] ReAct's ablation against Inner-Monologue-style prompts (ReAct-IM, replaying dense external feedback with no internal goal reasoning) shows internal sparse reasoning beats pure external-feedback replay: 71% vs 53% overall ALFWorld success — direct evidence about which reflection content adds value (goal-level re-planning) vs not (regurgitating observations) (ReAct §4).
  links: 83f1964c6217fb0d, 9240d1f949e33e15, 45e4bccf846fa0ba, ec766cdefb66d42e, 8eb9a877c6a47673, f4e7be8b62f08af8, 0369c5606fbf5ccc, d84e1b455ff7763a, 1f6daa3b273092cd
- `f26ea5ec230f7ffb` [repair] Unenforceable format rules (measured): IFEval-FC adherence ceiling <80% overall, Opus 4.1 letter-frequency 28%; nested/hierarchical output constraints far worse — IFHierBench best model 53.7% prompt-level, every model drops 37–53 pp at the first nesting level (d=0→d=1), d=3 collapses to ≤6% for 5/7 models; instruction stacking: follow-rate 96%→20–60% at 20 stacked instructions, 'output JSON' jointly unsatisfiable with 9 others.
  links: 4d082f928948911f, 9c53a0de2e7964bf, 22afd8a8bede9bbe, 308c11e281ffbca4, 0f3c6731224b1b90, 8eb9a877c6a47673, a3d101849aff988d, 1ab99428a950f6a4, 5f7aa50430ec256f
- `02a02d256e8ffada` [general] Aegis taxonomy (Song et al., U. Toronto/Vector; arXiv 2508.19504) defines 6 agent-environment failure modes in 3 categories: Exploration Failures (State-space Navigation Failure, State Awareness Failure), Exploitation Failures (Tool Output Processing Failure split into comparison/calculation/retrieval/sorting, Domain Rule Violation distinguishing invalid action vs. lack of correct action, User Instruction Following Failure), plus Resource Exhaustion (turn/token limit).
  links: 8479b0749854fe46, aa17692c22a78738, 38956b8103d9e760, f4b23532d8f51878, d0a70761a296b570, d897024c3f240a4b, 9ef58d49f849ae77, 89d1caf3454ccaea
- `0ff9b686f9e72fc3` [strategy] Beads ledger semantics (Gas Town): each unit of work is a bead (atomic, durable, version-controlled); polecats are workers with persistent identity and ephemeral sessions; a witness patrols each rig; a refinery serializes merges so nothing collides; a mayor coordinates across rigs; stamps are multi-dimensional attestations (quality/reliability/creativity) from validators that accrue into a portable character sheet — reputation derived from real work, not self-reported.
  links: a602df8d9b46c6b5, 23db60932cbf376c, bbbe2b85e6d0db1a, 3d5b6db5918b465b, f54b0ed68eef1372, b91eeab1a9452dee, 1691fdd86c2e2438, b4ee49de2637dd9e
- `104c7db4c0a0fa2a` [general] JSONSchemaBench (Geng et al., arXiv:2501.10868) measured empirical schema coverage on ~10K real-world schemas with Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct (greedy, 2-shot): LM-only compliance 0.90 (GlaiveAI), 0.65 (GitHub-Easy), 0.56 (Kubernetes), 0.46 (Snowplow), 0.38 (GitHub-Medium), 0.13 (GitHub-Hard), 0.21 (JSONSchemaStore). Prompting-only compliance collapses to 13–46% on medium-to-hard schemas; these are lower bounds for weak models, not frontier models.
  links: e88eb9f15941f838, a5b5c576f4ffd0b5, 0503a43026e3c7bc, 39c4a84aa83578bb, 10c72d909b09ad6b, 9edd522ac96ebcbc, 06cc8dfc000757fa, 01f00e1e3835a627
- `135c69977d75322e` [general] Hybrid LLM router latency overhead is negligible relative to generation: DeBERTa router 0.036±0.002 s/query vs Llama-2 13b generation 14.61±0.27 s (router ~10× faster than the fastest LLM, FLAN-t5 800m at 0.46 s). (Hybrid LLM, arXiv 2404.14618v1, §4.4, Table 2)
  links: 53b44257c13294e1, 6977145a51d83491, e151279bfa369f9a, 74a333f06b0e1b1b, ef6a82f7cb4bf553, 79088f70c96759ad, 536b91e7628f3054, 0a6c3cc372c5cf77
- `19ef42b700d36e05` [general] RecMem quality: selective batch consolidation matches or beats eager systems on accuracy — best overall among memory-based methods on both benchmarks (LoCoMo GPT-4.1-mini: 81.10 vs Mem0 62.92 / A-Mem 68.83; LongMemEval-S: 76.80 vs 71.20 / 71.60, and beats FullContext 66.20); but on short LoCoMo conversations FullContext still slightly outperforms RecMem (76.43 vs 72.47, gpt-4o-mini), i.e. the consolidation saving is not free on small contexts (RecMem Tables 1-2).
  links: 229361857af8028c, dd3d81d3e6c5124a, ca8c6940f3cb0005, 27cb143596b061e2, f80a8893ffad1994, fe6a2d93dd65d35c, 2bc3ff3548b14a6f, a101c8e7ede3b6b3
- `30d49ce68046cd2f` [general] Zep caveats the paper itself reports: on DMR Zep scores 94.8% vs MemGPT's 93.4% but the full-conversation baseline scored 94.4% (gpt-4-turbo) and 98.0% (gpt-4o-mini), so the DMR gap is within noise; Zep declined on single-session-assistant questions (−17.7% gpt-4o, −9.06% gpt-4o-mini) [S5] §4.2, §4.3.2.
  links: 87f0150ad5e164b8, ca79380b2a16a6e8, 9fd0d62f3cea36ac, 8de41cd967fdda17, 672b4912c42144b2, d32f5cbbb9dfef2a, 2bc3ff3548b14a6f, 15116661844082ce
- `31afd4aa76fb215a` [general] Recommended attributes on the inference span include gen_ai.conversation.compacted, gen_ai.request.frequency_penalty, gen_ai.request.max_tokens, gen_ai.request.presence_penalty, gen_ai.request.previous_response.id, gen_ai.request.reasoning.level, gen_ai.request.stop_sequences, gen_ai.request.temperature, gen_ai.request.top_p, gen_ai.response.finish_reasons, gen_ai.response.id, gen_ai.response.model, gen_ai.response.time_to_first_chunk (streaming), token-usage attributes gen_ai.usage.input_tokens/output_tokens/reasoning.output_tokens/cache_creation.input_tokens/cache_read.input_tokens, plus server.address.
  links: 3912dd0a304826cb, b1b91935c25b467a, 4c19d9b8fed90b2c, 2e18ebad8d315465, 7baa4e0fc447c393, 0a74dadc333efd53, a374d61735af78e8, 45e4bccf846fa0ba
- `33f44ff98f2913eb` [general] Uncertain/contested: whether 'self-reflection' per se (vs plain retry/re-sampling) contributes beyond the verification gate — Renze & Guven found even 'Retry' helped, a 2026 preprint finds a noise placebo matches self-correction on math, and another finds the 'can't self-correct' result is partly a chat-template role-labeling artifact; these are not yet reconciled. Renze & Guven's positive MCQA result and Huang et al.'s negative reasoning result are not comparable protocols (correct-answer-conditioned reflection + re-answer vs intrinsic correction).
  links: a375ddea979ace49, ab49a330f50c49ef, 399d42c5feedc012, 0cae2c2bdf2ef1dc, 888b4004f537880f, 6b4813b0dee8f914, 635c8cbfd002fa86, bcac82d5414851c8
- `40a6f5c1783caef7` [general] OpenAI Agents SDK session guide assigns: SQLiteSession to local development and simple apps, AsyncSQLiteSession to async dev, RedisSession to shared session memory across multiple workers or services, SQLAlchemySession to production apps with existing databases (PostgreSQL, MySQL, SQLite, etc.), MongoDBSession to multi-process horizontally-scalable session storage, and DaprSession to production cloud-native deployments; the documented production path for concurrent workers is a shared-server DB or a single long-running app server that serializes access, not a shared SQLite file.
  links: 08460d217f2ef14e, 8fa553364e85a282, 0ed192e5705ef919, 5025c0afbe426e17, abc0df15df514356, 2b319322a0428380, 7b02843dca2475f6, 28a317b7a861c29c
- `53b44257c13294e1` [general] Hybrid LLM routes each query to exactly one of two models (large vs small), never calling multiple LLMs per query. The router is a BERT-style encoder (DeBERTa-v3-large) trained to predict the quality gap H(x)=q(S(x))−q(L(x)) where q is BART score; a test-time threshold on the router score trades quality for cost. (Hybrid LLM, arXiv 2404.14618v1, §2.2, §3.1-3.3)
  links: cef70c90b6220520, 6977145a51d83491, 135c69977d75322e, feeed923077d1e1f, e9faf4c799927644, 7724947e09734d4a, 4ca81b227fadc600, 84c8cabac4c26d5f
- `5d32c1280761f55f` [general] BigQuery incremental eligibility is strict: incremental views support a limited feature set; AVG, ARRAY_AGG, and APPROX_COUNT_DISTINCT results are stored as intermediate sketches rather than final values; a deletion in a base table can force a full refresh (notably for BigLake sources) because the partition layout is what permits update-in-place.
  links: 266b8325088b1614, 60fdbe45190d2e94, 15355f27b297c061, ae4ee7c4c7369b1f, 6dbd271b7ce0e25e, 8ab263a3dc129e33, e761f79662ed051f, a2d0d1644fe1d3d4
- `6b4813b0dee8f914` [general] Established synthesis: pure intrinsic self-correction (no external feedback) on reasoning adds ≈0 or negative value — Huang et al., Self-Refine's GSM8K ≈0pp, Reflexion's test-omission ablation (0.52 < 0.60 base), and the 2026 stability-threshold preprint (only 3 of 7 tested frontier models non-degrading).
  links: ab49a330f50c49ef, 0b60fb3ea9f2562a, 9240d1f949e33e15, 8fe5600483b3642b, 33f44ff98f2913eb, 635c8cbfd002fa86, f103d9ef613d250f, 293c648eb7e6dfe0
- `73649fc34b71a4f6` [general] ESTIMATE (vendor design guidance, no published numbers in the sources reached): the scaling curve implied by LangGraph's and OpenAI's backend menus is roughly in-memory → SQLite file → Postgres/Redis/MongoDB, but neither publishes a benchmark for agent state at scale in the cited pages.
  links: 8fa553364e85a282, 0ed192e5705ef919, 3b3bae3fd783ee40, 7b02843dca2475f6, 330187df00ed1779, 28a317b7a861c29c, f1bbc7cc63d0bf3b, a2d0d1644fe1d3d4
- `7b02843dca2475f6` [general] OpenAI Agents SDK built-in session backends: SQLiteSession is the default for 'local development and simple apps'; documented shared/multi-process paths are RedisSession ('shared memory across workers… low-latency distributed deployments'), SQLAlchemySession (Postgres etc.), MongoDBSession ('horizontally-scalable, multi-process session storage'), and DaprSession. Operational guidance: in-memory SQLite for temporary, file-based SQLite for persistent, Redis for shared low-latency, MongoDB for multi-process horizontally-scalable storage.
  links: 40a6f5c1783caef7, 8fa553364e85a282, 0ed192e5705ef919, abc0df15df514356, 73649fc34b71a4f6, 28a317b7a861c29c, f54b0ed68eef1372, e8df321a0ca42231
- `7c0a290e885b0a7d` [general] Agent memory systems almost universally keep the long-term fact store append-only and unbounded, solving 'growth' by bounding only the in-context tier and by re-ranking/superseding at retrieval: MemGPT/Letta evicts the context queue to a recursive summary while archival/recall storage grows indefinitely; decay exists as a retrieval-time ranking bias and never deletes; dedup is write-time; invalidation/supersede is timestamp-based and non-destructive; per-item expiry exists as soft-hide (Mem0 expiration_date) and is absent from LangGraph's core store.
  links: 3997ff446a6ec077, af4621ee1fa4b90c, 90b6a3e4a78524a2, 43f4539098e18753, 1cebb877f366157c, 19d6b1929b157a24, a495d4c500ec01b5, 15116661844082ce
- `9f7bbdd4b6a5377b` [general] Guided decoding in real RAG workloads (Newmind, arXiv:2509.06631): 0-turn FP rates 0.49–3.20% across Outlines/XGrammar/LM Format Enforcer; multi-turn prompting cuts FP rates to as low as 0.06–0.30%. Backend matters at scale: in a 10M-chunk/100K-query extrapolation, replacing LM Format Enforcer with XGrammar cost ~1,600–4,000 (Qwen) / 134–2,000 (Llama) additional missed references; latency roughly backend-independent (30–51 s/sample). 'Lowest FP' and 'best recall' do not always pick the same backend.
  links: e88eb9f15941f838, 18005e6da326e5fc, 2df44a2e25263275, 6eb2a6b0a6c49c71, a4951002397ee060, 824c39e752901ec2, 06cc8dfc000757fa, 0a6c3cc372c5cf77
- `a375ddea979ace49` [general] Training reflection into weights (rather than prompting it) shows large gains: SPOC (2025, preprint) — single-pass interleaved solution+verification model via SFT + online RL improved Llama-3.1-8B/70B by +8.8/+11.6pp on MATH500, +10.0/+20.0pp on AMC23, +3.3/+6.7pp on AIME24. S2R (2025, preprint) — teaching self-verification + self-correction via SFT and outcome/process RL lifted Qwen2.5-Math-7B from 51.0% to 81.6% with only 3.1k seed samples (arXiv:2506.06923; arXiv:2502.12853; both arXiv preprints).
  links: 399d42c5feedc012, 0cae2c2bdf2ef1dc, 888b4004f537880f, 8fe5600483b3642b, 33f44ff98f2913eb, edfc9ea838f66df0, 635c8cbfd002fa86, 84c8cabac4c26d5f
- `abee484fca1be8a6` [evidence-status] mini-agi `disagrees` is a first-class outcome: verifier pass == claims achieved -> `verified`/`verified-failed`, otherwise `disagrees`; it is a judge-calibration signal with exit 1, and `loop verify` closes a gap only when the composite reaches the target AND the verifier passes — a self-reported outcome is not trusted when a verifier is available and disagrees.
  links: 20775a40371d1e55, 7fe943345da11d30, 8af8f2689671ac0a, 439ade46d55d5c15, 467e731dc67aed05, 8fe5600483b3642b, b06e866e7dd44247, 2cafe6061a29c299
- `cb3c088728b3c87c` [general] 'Evidence Lock Before Commitment' (Singh, arXiv:2608.05353, verified in full text): the judge's interface matters — freezing a judge's extracted evidence as the exclusive input to the verdict call hurts. Across 24,000 judgments (1,500 comparisons x 4 protocols x 2 answer orders x 2 judges — Claude Sonnet 4.5, GPT-5; HelpSteer3/FeedbackQA/CoVal): evidence-locking reduces preference agreement by 4.3 pp (Claude)/5.6 pp (GPT-5) vs structured one-call judging (71.7/70.0% vs 76.0/75.6%) and increases answer-order inconsistency by 9.5/8.0 points; pointwise locking is worse (67.3/69.2%); structured one-call evidence elicitation stays close to standard judging (75.3-76.0%) — the harm is the frozen, exclusive record, not asking for evidence. Locked judging costs far more (mean completion tokens 1,279 → 4,183 evidence-lock → 6,825 pointwise). Removing source access degrades the downstream decision by 4-6 pp and doubles order sensitivity — auditability should be additive (record + source), not a frozen replacement.
  links: e232f1c2ce57f2cf, c1a0b768075c2467, edac6d9654eefceb, 45e4bccf846fa0ba, 61d5bd996f9f2ad9, f4e7be8b62f08af8, baf9ded88a16fc68, ce00079fe0159247
- `cf94cceed826d20c` [general] LongMemEval design deltas under growth: fact-augmented key expansion improves memory recall@k by 9.4% and QA accuracy by 5.4%; time-aware query expansion improves temporal-reasoning recall by 6.8-11.3%; Chain-of-Note plus structured format improves QA accuracy by up to 10 absolute points.

- `dca21576c4c8bae6` [general] Zep (arXiv:2501.13956 Table 3) time-aware invalidation improves LongMemEval knowledge-update and temporal-reasoning categories vs full-context reading: with gpt-4o, knowledge-update 78.2% to 83.3% and temporal-reasoning 45.1% to 62.4%.
  links: b6fef0270ed8d28d, 8718ee8dce4540b4, 2c6fb8ddf6a41794, abb91cc1c7c944cc, 68944cdb3db0a2fc, cf94cceed826d20c, 348e2784f9115e96, d32f5cbbb9dfef2a
- `e761f79662ed051f` [general] Transferable patterns for regenerating derived documents from append-only sources (source constraints are fact; the generalization is opinion): pure/deterministic derivation, a durable change/append log plus high-watermark position, keyed upsert for idempotent replay, incremental state stored adjacent to the output, an explicit full-rebuild path for when logic changes, and bounded staleness with in-place swap or merge.
  links: ecca3611145516e6, 7b3058140a429de1, c436a95d905a7aa0, 15355f27b297c061, 5d32c1280761f55f, 16f2c921fa480440, 8ab263a3dc129e33, 53591a59c549819a
- `e8df321a0ca42231` [general] Verdict (uncertain): the 'one shared brain vs per-project instances' tradeoff has no controlled measurement in any reached primary source; the documented rationale is qualitative (context degradation, illustrative startup token costs, RAG-index staleness) and operational guidance uniformly favors many small instances; a concrete per-task or per-repo memory architecture (e.g., canonical-memory kernel) is not a documented pattern in any of the four systems' official docs.
  links: 3b3bae3fd783ee40, 7b02843dca2475f6, 4e221bfa0c5d7bc3, f1bbc7cc63d0bf3b, 0f1a1f2be8e214d4, e7777e2701a56d30, 07b0d711ef3fc303, d39c4dd366a95d2e
- `ecda50a3261745b3` [general] No published batch-vs-continuous head-to-head exists: all measured quality/cost differences in [S1]–[S7] are within-system comparisons against full-context or RAG baselines; none of the sources reports the same system under both nightly-batch and continuous consolidation; the closest evidence for the trade-off is Zep's design claim that continuous dynamic extension trades graph quality (periodic refresh needed) for latency/LLM cost [S5] §2.3.
  links: a1cb6948c6bd72ed, 75149e672ee23e31, 56ffb2187d17cbe9, 8718ee8dce4540b4, 0a3013221a215aa2, 3158dabb8476e655, 15b82401938c5a93, 3e2267fb03bf9e44
- `f02881fc4dc7a2ea` [general] Autogrammar (arXiv:2608.05493) learns context-free grammars from documentation + execution logs for low-resource DSLs. Motivation: across 416 AWS services, 23% expose a string parameter requiring a DSL and AWS uses ≥59 unique DSLs. Measured precision on unseen data: 0.999 / 1.00 / 0.910 (CWIL/DQL/DSS) vs Arvada (SOTA oracle-based grammar induction) 0.025 on CWIL despite ~63k oracle calls / ~14.6 h, and vs direct-LM baselines (0.023–0.510) an order of magnitude below. Ablations: execution data is crucial, documentation largely dispensable; LTL constraints cut learning time 3.8x with no significant precision loss. End-to-end GCD loop improves LM success on 8/10 real CloudWatch tasks and matches a professionally-maintained grammar. The GCD grammar-availability constraint is removable — it becomes a data-availability problem, not a grammar-authoring problem.
  links: adbc0cde1ae47dc7, 4cb71d0255cccf49, 4d082f928948911f, 7724947e09734d4a, b06e866e7dd44247, fe15f8a0ae73fa1f, 04ce20f686503fa3, 07bdd35766db54d4
- `f4b23532d8f51878` [general] Longitudinal silent-failure taxonomy (Wu; arXiv 2606.14589, 'When Errors Become Narratives') defines five mechanism-oriented classes of agent silent failures: (A) environment and platform quirks, (B) design-assumption mismatches, (C) error swallowing and dilution, (D) chained hallucination and fabrication (described as the unique-to-LLM 'fail-plausible' pattern), (E) operational omission and forensic blind spots.
  links: 984129939642011a, 02a02d256e8ffada, d897024c3f240a4b, 554b0217976296b3, 9ef58d49f849ae77, c1a0b768075c2467, 4cf12f9a8fbae069, ef69e42494b930cc
- `f54b0ed68eef1372` [general] Mapping to mini-agi: Beads ledger ~ checkpoint journal (memory/episodic/checkpoints.log) + evals/results run ledger + memory/canonical facts; Refinery ~ planner::finalize_and_merge; Witness ~ bg worker supervision / loop run watchdog; Polecat ~ bg workers with persistent session identity (MINIAGI_SESSION_TAG, detached runs, respawns); Stamp ~ eval gate multi-dim score (composite, outcome, cost_usd, tokens, tool_mismatches); persistent state ~ run-state index (status::index_runs).

- `0a74dadc333efd53` [general] gen_ai.server.request.duration metric: unit s; required gen_ai.operation.name and gen_ai.provider.name; conditional error.type, gen_ai.request.model, server.port; recommended gen_ai.response.model and server.address.
  links: 4c19d9b8fed90b2c, 31afd4aa76fb215a, 2e18ebad8d315465, 7baa4e0fc447c393, a374d61735af78e8, 55a5de088bb8a4cb, 176d8ed99f2c76c6
- `0b6bcd41fb0c4fef` [general] Retrieval reading strategy is a separate measurable lever: LongMemEval, even with perfect retrieval, Chain-of-Note + structured data format improves QA accuracy by as much as 10 absolute points across three LLMs; on LongMemEvalS long-context LLMs drop 30-60% vs oracle retrieval. Source: arXiv:2410.10813v2, Section 5.5 + Figure 3b.

- `0ed192e5705ef919` [general] Unverified/opinion: whether a single SQLite file is a viable store for many concurrent sandboxed workers is not documented by any vendor as a blessed topology; production frameworks instead document sharing via Redis/Postgres/MongoDB/Dapr or a single server, with SQLite reserved for dev/local.
  links: 40a6f5c1783caef7, 8fa553364e85a282, 7b02843dca2475f6, 73649fc34b71a4f6, 28a317b7a861c29c, b023dfa34d7b6603, a2d0d1644fe1d3d4
- `10c72d909b09ad6b` [general] lm-evaluation-harness integration of JSONSchemaBench defines json_validity (syntax) and schema_compliance (jsonschema package) as independent binary metrics; its worked example for Llama-3.2-1B on medium: json_validity 1.0, schema_compliance 0.2 — 100% parseable JSON but 80% of outputs violate the schema, so a syntax-level retry-on-parse-error loop would not trigger while a schema-validator retry loop fires on 80% of draws.
  links: 104c7db4c0a0fa2a, 2df44a2e25263275, 473e91ddc0c06971, 39c4a84aa83578bb, d84e1b455ff7763a, 824c39e752901ec2, 06cc8dfc000757fa
- `20775a40371d1e55` [evidence-status] mini-agi verifier (crates/mini-agi-core/src/verifier.rs) runs `sh -c "<verify_command>"` with `current_dir(target_path)` and maps exit code vs claimed outcome: `verified` (gate passed AND run claims achieved), `verified-failed` (gate failed AND run claims failed), `disagrees` (mismatch), `unverified` (no verifier declared). A 120s timeout kills the process and reports `disagrees`.
  links: 7fe943345da11d30, ac9b554f4139828d, abee484fca1be8a6, 8af8f2689671ac0a, d5728fa1ece59eee, 439ade46d55d5c15, 467e731dc67aed05
- `2372968ceac568ce` [general] Temporal reasoning is the single hardest fact-recall category across agent-memory benchmarks: LoCoMo human temporal F1=92.6 vs best base model GPT-4-turbo=10.4; long-context LLMs lag human levels especially in temporal reasoning by 73%. Source: arXiv:2402.17753, Table 2 + Section 6.

- `28a317b7a861c29c` [general] All three major frameworks route production durability to databases, not flat files: LangGraph to Postgres ('Ideal for using in production'), OpenAI Agents SDK to Redis/SQLAlchemy/MongoDB/Dapr for 'shared', 'distributed', 'horizontally-scalable' memory, Microsoft to Durable Task external storage for 'multi-instance scale-out.'

- `39c4a84aa83578bb` [general] Retry pressure from JSONSchemaBench (estimate derived from their reported compliance): expected draws to first compliant output = 1/compliance — LM-only GitHub-Hard ≈ 7.7, Outlines JSONSchemaStore ≈ 4.2, Guidance GitHub-Hard ≈ 1.45. The provider 'no retries' claim holds only where declared coverage is non-zero; on GitHub-Hard OpenAI's declared coverage of 0.09 means 91% of schemas would be rejected/unsupported rather than retried against.

- `50662f026de1acfe` [general] Embedding-based retrieval alone does not close the gap to human fact recall in long conversations: on LongMemEval even commercial memory chatbots with dense memory (ChatGPT GPT-4o 57.7%, Coze 33.0%) drop 37% and 64% vs offline reading of full context (91.8%). Source: arXiv:2410.10813v2, Figure 3a.

- `57ed22f30f0c6760` [general] SSRF during OAuth metadata discovery: servers can point resource_metadata/authorization_servers/token endpoints at cloud metadata (169.254.169.254), localhost, or private ranges; mitigations are HTTPS-only (except loopback in dev), blocking private/link-local/loopback ranges (RFC 9728 7.7), egress proxies, and DNS-pinning (Security Best Practices).

- `73454769bc163ce3` [repair] TCMIIES (Zhao, arXiv:2605.07507; system report, verified in full text): schema-guided-prompting extraction (no constrained decoding, commercial LLM APIs) with a concurrent batch engine carrying up to three automatic retries (1 s delay) per record measured on 500 CNKI TCM papers × 3 providers achieved 94.2% average structured-output compliance (DeepSeek v4-flash 95.8, Qwen turbo 94.2, GLM-4 flash 92.5).

- `77ad6114bf5546c2` [ai-auditability] Established: each mechanism is a real, standards-/law-backed practice with distinct content — decision logs (ADR/KEP rationale + versioning, OWASP when/who/what fields and separate audit trails), evidence chains (PROV attribution/derivation; in-toto/SLSA digest-bound signed attestations; VC Data Integrity proof sets/chains and proof purposes), approval records (EU AI Act mandatory human-oversight and biometric-authorisation approvals recorded in system logs with six-month retention; NIST AI RMF Playbook documented roles, standardized model documentation, and inventory).

- `999b1d884e08eb37` [general] Hybrid indexing (multiple keys per memory item) improves agent-memory recall: LongMemEval key expansion (K = value + extracted user facts) over K = value gives +9.4% avg recall@k and +5.4% end-to-end QA accuracy; key merging ≫ post-retrieval rank merging (Table 10). Source: arXiv:2410.10813v2, Section 5.3, Tables 3, 10.

- `b4ee49de2637dd9e` [general] Verdict for mini-agi: core kernel ideas (durable ledger, serializing merge, watchdog, multi-dim eval) are already held; the differentiated adoptions worth pursuing are (1) a unified queryable ledger view and (4) per-rig witness semantics — both incremental, kernel-scoped, and matching the charter — while federation (Wasteland) and declarative SDK (Gas City) are out of scope for a single-binary kernel.

- `e368dbd927efdfc0` [general] MemGPT (Packer et al., arXiv:2310.08560, 2023) reports handling contexts exceeding the LLM context window by a factor of 10 while maintaining performance on dialogue and document analysis tasks, via OS-inspired tiered memory (main/external context) with selective retrieval ('paging'). The quantitative claim is the 10x context-window multiplier, not a directly measured token-cost reduction; token saving is the implied mechanism. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.08560

- `e6b84c294456ecd4` [general] Google denotes a test flaky so it reports failure only if it fails 3 times in a row, reducing false positives but encouraging developers to ignore flakiness; Google also supports re-running only failing tests and automatic re-run when tests fail. (John Micco, Google Testing Blog, 2016)

- `0e8c9801a605c550` [general] LongMemEval findings: long-context LLMs drop 30–60% vs. oracle retrieval on LongMemEval_S; commercial ChatGPT and Coze dropped 37% and 64% vs. offline reading; ChatGPT tended to overwrite crucial information as chat continues, while Coze often failed to record indirectly provided user information.

- `0f1a1f2be8e214d4` [general] Every behavioral practice covered — warnings-deny in CI, small unsafe blocks, SAFETY comments, Result over panic, sealed traits, property-testing as a complement, toolchain pinning, the CI channel matrix — appears in primary sources as expert/official guidance supported by stated rationale, not by controlled or comparative measurement; none of the sources cites a study measuring defect-reduction effects.

- `1a44ed072b912b06` [general] Consistency has a price on the write path: SQL Server DML query performance can degrade significantly on tables referenced by indexed views; pg_ivm immediate maintenance slows base-table writes and raises errors in REPEATABLE READ/SERIALIZABLE for multi-base-table views under concurrent updates; Oracle ON COMMIT may increase commit time.

- `32bed97e81d99774` [evidence-status] SWE-bench counts `resolved` only when the post-patch repo passes the required tests, so the oracle can disagree with a solved-looking patch. The quantitative wrong-but-passing-patch numbers (~28.5% of a SWE-bench sample passes a Docker-verified incorrect patch; 7.8% of counted-correct patches fail the developer suite; one-in-five 'solved' patches semantically wrong) are estimates reported only in mini-agi's research doc citing preprints (2606.16062, 2503.15223, 2603.00520) that were not fetched — unconfirmed, flagged preprint; treat as calibration targets only after replication.

- `4c19d9b8fed90b2c` [general] Conditionally required attributes on the inference span: error.type (if error), gen_ai.conversation.id (when available), gen_ai.output.type (when output format requested), gen_ai.prompt.name/gen_ai.prompt.version (when named template used), gen_ai.request.choice.count, gen_ai.request.model (if available), gen_ai.request.seed, gen_ai.request.stream, gen_ai.request.top_k, server.port (if server.address set).
  links: 8402661c95f9d43c, 31afd4aa76fb215a, 2e18ebad8d315465, 0a74dadc333efd53, a374d61735af78e8, 5f7aa50430ec256f
- `4c4453fd5125f78d` [general] JSON Whisperer (arXiv:2510.04717) — patch-based JSON repair instead of regeneration: LLM emits an RFC 6902 JSON Patch. Two measured failure modes of naive patch generation: missing related updates (fragmented patch generation causes models to miss necessary updates) and array index arithmetic ('even state-of-the-art models frequently miscalculate these shifts or conflate zero-based and one-based indexing'). Fix EASE (Explicitly Addressed Sequence Encoding) replaces positional arrays with dictionaries of stable keys plus a list_display_order string. Measured: patch generation with EASE matches full-regeneration quality within 5% while cutting tokens 31% (output 42% smaller), 42.3% lower latency, 42.5% lower cost. Few-shot (synthetic examples) is what makes patch generation work at all. Structured edits have their own failure modes distinct from structured generation.

- `6665ad2250b0bf6a` [general] The three approaches (WAL+checkpoint, snapshot-restore, event sourcing) are one family, not alternatives: every credible system (PostgreSQL, SQLite, WiredTiger/MongoDB, RocksDB, etcd/Raft, Temporal, LangGraph, AWS Step Functions) combines an append-only, checksummed, order-preserving log with periodic state snapshots that bound replay.

- `6e30b9e0905d102a` [general] Established multi-source finding: showing a calibrated confidence score helps operators when the score is the operative mechanism — it improved trust calibration (Zhang 2020), machine and human confidence each significantly improved hybrid human+AI accuracy within a narrow accuracy band (Steyvers 2022), and uncertainty-based abstention improved deployed clinical accuracy ~20-25 points (Wienholt 2026).

- `70c12cac8fc7ae06` [repair] Melo (Wang et al., NetEase Cloud Music, arXiv:2607.23718; RecSys '26; production system report): music-recommendation agent behind Muse Mix (100M+ user platform, >1M cumulative users) built as a deterministic five-node state graph (UNDERSTAND→PLAN→EXECUTE→REFLECT→SYNTHESIZE) deliberately not a free-form ReAct loop, because 'across a free-form chain, intent-parsing, argument-extraction, and tool-side errors compounded across steps'; named-node defenses: inference-time entity grounding (L1 catalog reverse-lookup, L2 prompt-level consumption rules, L3 plan-time guards) and reflective retry (REFLECT→PLAN back edge, capped at two rounds, choosing among proceed / retry_rewrite / retry_relaxed / fallback).

[fact]

- `9a41828e542b1154` [general] Codex project identity is gated by trust: Codex loads project-scoped config files only when the project is trusted; if untrusted it ignores project .codex/ layers including .codex/config.toml, project-local hooks, and project-local rules; project-scoped config cannot override credential/provider/telemetry keys.

- `9edd522ac96ebcbc` [repair] SchemaBench (no enforcement, frontier, ~40K schemas, depth 16.7; measured): GPT-4o overall valid-JSON 61.06% (complex 84.47% / custom 61.56% / escape 37.14%), GPT-4o-mini 43.98%; parser-error floors 8% (GPT-4o), 23% (Llama-3.2-3B), 36% (MiniCPM) — native generation fails on roughly 40–90% of complex schemas; the two big benchmarks disagree on the no-enforcement regime (JSONSchemaBench 13–46% vs SchemaBench 27–61%) due to schema-depth and model differences, so neither generalizes to 'the' rate (uncertain / not verifiable).

- `b8b7f1d1340fadc7` [repair] Agent-level reliability plateaus (measured): τ-bench gpt-4o succeeds on <50% of policy-compliant tool tasks and pass^8 is <25% in retail — 8 retries still fail on >75% of tasks, so pure resampling is exhausted; live BFCL V4 top model (Claude-Opus-4-5 FC) is 77.47% overall, multi-turn the weak category (best 68.38%); robustness drops: toolkit expansion with semantically related tools −8 to −19%, query rephrase −1 to −8%.

- `d1b3d3ef01bfcfff` [ai-auditability] Opinion: the weakness of a bare decision log is integrity and non-repudiation — OWASP states plain logs cannot reliably provide it without signatures; the evidence-chain standards supply exactly the missing machinery (signed Statements bound to subjects by digest, provenance predicates, proof chains). The suggested shape is a decision log whose entries embed digest references bound by an attestation/evidence chain, with approval records as human-signed gates on top — not one mechanism chosen over the others.

- `d71aa36a9e81bc8c` [general] Seccomp is a syscall filter: a BPF program evaluated on struct seccomp_data (syscall number, arch, instruction_pointer, up to 6 raw register arguments) deciding KILL/TRAP/ERRNO/USER_NOTIF/TRACE/LOG/ALLOW. BPF cannot dereference pointers, which kernel docs credit with making the filter itself immune to the TOCTOU problems of syscall interposition frameworks (source: seccomp_filter.rst, seccomp(2)).

- `d7e7994431d530e1` [repair] Retry/refinement economics (measured): sampling diversity saturates (only 1.2→2.5 unique reasoning clusters from N=2→32 on AIME-24); refining each sampled trajectory (self-critique→self-correct→majority vote, no verifier) beats both plain resampling and verifier-based best-of-N (Qwen2.5-1.5B MATH500 29.6→58.0, AMC 25.0→32.5, +28 pp); under tool-environment unreliability recovery quality beats retry quantity — targeted recovery hints recover more than test-time scaling (ToolBench-X; agents fail under 5 recoverable hazards despite good reliable-tool performance).
  links: 4626517e46b1c8e3, 90b87f746aa7223d, b4ac4344ace6c5a2, dc2abae1015a10fc, 61d5bd996f9f2ad9, 1150dfe53f759651
- `d86f43007ae0e974` [general] The SQLite wal-index is backed by a file mmapped in the same directory as the database; processes with a different root directory (via chroot) will see different files and use different shared memory areas, leading to database corruption — a chroot'd sandbox worker cannot reliably share a WAL-mode store on the same filesystem path.
  links: 510db6dc2a1720ad, 3758f98022d19fbb, 221140d4511ffebf, 3e5e18d029069f90, c65a4ce6cdb624b8, 15b82401938c5a93
- `e1f2e874ff8f4e1d` [general] Oracle fast refresh applies to the materialized view only the changes to master tables recorded in materialized view logs; COMPLETE refresh re-executes the defining query; FORCE (default) does fast refresh if possible else complete refresh.
  links: 8097fca3fceebe45, 6268ad4cbce610b0, 19fdc143f081ed4f, cfddc08e47e6dcf7, 266b8325088b1614, ae4ee7c4c7369b1f
- `e232f1c2ce57f2cf` [general] MCP enforced contract: `inputSchema` MUST be a valid JSON Schema object (2020-12 default); `outputSchema` is optional but binding when present (servers MUST provide conforming structured results, clients SHOULD validate); tool annotations from non-trusted servers MUST be considered untrusted; servers MUST validate all inputs, enforce access controls, rate-limit, and sanitize outputs; clients SHOULD validate tool results before handing them to the LLM; there SHOULD be a human able to deny invocations; SEP-1303 routes input-validation failures into the `isError: true` tool-result channel.
  links: ddf4bfc7760221e4, 728334f20b68d83d, 854c0680bf360524, 97cf04e1f8130ba2, cb3c088728b3c87c, e5a6d2ccb1b2bd7a
- `e4344628bee35e4c` [general] OAuth authorization URL validation: servers MUST only allow http/https (https in production), reject dangerous schemes (javascript:/data:/file:), never open URLs via shell, and apply CSP script-src 'self' (Security Best Practices).
  links: c81957cf3aac4c06, af0d9ca54cec9c18, 57ed22f30f0c6760, 9eba1c8e9d2d65b7, 0d9ab0996bcbca80, 9a9c35587bb37962
- `e7777e2701a56d30` [general] Anthropic's documented guidance favors more, smaller instances: if Claude was corrected more than twice on the same issue in one session, /clear and start fresh with a more specific prompt; subagents exist so file reads stay in a separate context (worked example: subagent reading ~6,100 tokens of files returns a ~420-token summary).

- `fe6a2d93dd65d35c` [general] Measured cost of an explicit batch consolidation pass: LightMem's offline 'sleep-time' OP-update pass raises construction cost vs online-soft-update alone — e.g. LongMemEval-S GPT-4o-mini LightMem(r=0.7,th=512): online 28.25K tokens / 18.4 calls / 283.8s runtime -> with OP-update 83.44K / 125.5 calls / 496.0s; the batch pass roughly triples construction cost but stays ~14x cheaper than Mem0's eager construction (1,152.6K) (LightMem Table 2).
  links: 8718ee8dce4540b4, 27cb143596b061e2, f0cad0a342fce769, 26789dfb6ee09b3f, c5fada8b53377287, 19ef42b700d36e05
- `0006a8ef037932a1` [general] No public apples-to-apples benchmark runs the same agent traces through naive truncation vs LLM summarization vs compaction vs retrieval and reports fact-recall side by side; vendor numbers are directional evidence, not a ranking. What would settle it: a neutral benchmark with fixed transcripts, identical inputs across methods, and both downstream task success and fact-recall metrics.
  links: 0a3013221a215aa2, 348e2784f9115e96, 9fd23bfe4eb2af7f, 3b3bae3fd783ee40, 15116661844082ce
- `0b60fb3ea9f2562a` [general] A 2026 arXiv preprint (not yet peer-reviewed) recasts iterative self-correction as feedback control with a stability threshold (correct when error-correction-rate/error-introduction-rate exceeds a bound); across 7 models × {GSM8K, MATH, StrategyQA}, only o3-mini (+3.4pp), Claude Opus 4.6 (+0.6pp), and o4-mini (≈0) were non-degrading, while GPT-5 and four others lost accuracy; a verify-first prompt intervention turned GPT-4o-mini's −6.2pp degradation into +0.2pp (arXiv:2604.22273).
  links: 6b4813b0dee8f914, 8fe5600483b3642b, 8eb9a877c6a47673, 4ca81b227fadc600, 635c8cbfd002fa86
- `0d0774d31bed17b3` [general] Claude Code memory/instruction loading uses five scope tiers in explicit load order: managed policy, user (~/.claude/CLAUDE.md), project (./CLAUDE.md or ./.claude/CLAUDE.md), local (CLAUDE.local.md), plus per-directory files loaded on demand; discovered files are concatenated, not merged/overriding.
  links: 8eff920971cc7319, b7d8338a5de669c3, 9fac6494bbf5da78, d6482a9be6ea492f, 8f32a4d8dcbf2ca5
- `15355f27b297c061` [general] When dbt incremental logic changes, incremental results diverge from historical rows stored in the target table, so the model must be rebuilt via dbt run --full-refresh; the default on_schema_change policy is 'ignore', meaning a removed column makes the run fail.
  links: 266b8325088b1614, 33aa2b6ddca3a89e, ae4ee7c4c7369b1f, 5d32c1280761f55f, e761f79662ed051f
- `1b0ebc092cb2ebe8` [general] Unverified from sources reached: how and whether specific deployed vendors (ChatGPT, Claude, Vertex) present confidence to operators in production; several key claims (F7 verbalized confidence, F8 P(True), F11 self-consistency, F12 calibrated-must-hallucinate) rest on author abstracts rather than full texts; full texts of Lee & See 2004, Dietvorst 2015, and Yáñez 2026 were not read (title/metadata level only).
  links: 2cd8c890524c508b, 862b7661a840e726, 154306422881beb8, 6e30b9e0905d102a, a2d0d1644fe1d3d4
- `2469f7b7d49a226e` [evidence-status] Anthropic handles verifier disagreement by routing: in multi-agent research, a single LLM judge scored most consistent when eval test cases had a clear answer and the fix for edge cases was human evaluation plus prompt heuristics (source-quality), not a new scorer; Claude Code's Stop-hook gate has a bounded override (8 consecutive blocks ends the turn).
  links: 49e58f71d2bdd715, 3c50f0967b34b231, 8af8f2689671ac0a, 22abb140e3c15e39, d84e1b455ff7763a
- `2512869e54f72f96` [general] Conformal risk control can't certify structured-output correctness beyond the base error rate (arXiv:2606.29054): models report 74–97% average confidence while actual risk ranges 3% (JSON) to 80% (NQ Open); whenever base risk μ > target risk α, any distribution-free certifier must abstain on ≥ (μ−α)/(1−α) of examples (CoNLL NER 72B forces ≥16% abstention at α=0.10; 3B ≥36%; NQ Open ~78% — hard NER/QA/CLS are uncertifiable at α=0.10). The path to certification is reducing μ (model scale), not tighter bounds. JSON extraction is the easy case: μ≈0, certifies at all scales with zero abstention. Bound hierarchy: Hoeffding 8.7% < Bernstein 11.9% < betting e-CRC 12.2% certified of 656. Under distribution shift static guarantees fail (60–71% violation rates); ACI restores control in the feasible regime (to 4–21%). Honest rule: compute μ first, then choose the bound and score (Self-Consistency strongest, AUROC to 0.82; Token Margin best for JSON, 0.82).
  links: 6d07ba987c2ea213, c0fb76a443969f8b, b023dfa34d7b6603, cfe795ab8c642a7e, 20b4f75f5aab7cb5
- `3158dabb8476e655` [general] No primary source was found publishing a quantitative quarantine threshold (the Google post's 'too high' is unspecified) or an optimal retry-count trade-off; the verdict lists a published flakiness-rate threshold for auto-quarantine or a CI cost/retry study such as Leinen et al. 'Cost of Flaky Tests in Continuous Integration' (TUM/CQSE 2023) as evidence that would settle the thresholds.
  links: ecda50a3261745b3, 0a3013221a215aa2, e6b84c294456ecd4, fb857bcd7b9b0020, d2375864da7ee620
- `3997ff446a6ec077` [general] MemGPT consolidation is the queue manager's recursive summarization: on memory pressure (warning at ~70% of context) the agent self-edits working/archival memory; at flush (~100%) it evicts ~50% of the window and "generates a new recursive summary using the existing recursive summary and evicted messages" [S1] §2.2.
  links: dfa6bac8d91fe7f9, af4621ee1fa4b90c, 90b6a3e4a78524a2, ca79380b2a16a6e8, 7c0a290e885b0a7d
- `3e5e18d029069f90` [general] Over a network filesystem, SQLite warns that file-locking logic is buggy in many network filesystem implementations, two or more clients might modify the same part of the same database at the same time resulting in corruption, and there is nothing SQLite can do to prevent it.

- `439ade46d55d5c15` [testing] mini-agi's kernel defines a declared verifier as VACUOUS if it passes on an empty target and refuses to trust it; its audit runs the verifier on the known-good verify_target (gold check) AND on an empty temp directory (counterfactual check), reporting PASS only when the gate accepts the real target and rejects the empty one (crates/mini-agi-core/src/verifier.rs:163-239).
  links: 20775a40371d1e55, eb30bfc42085c15c, abee484fca1be8a6, 8af8f2689671ac0a, 40120af48e7950b9
- `457ddfbc8482528c` [repair] Schema representation as independent lever (measured, TSCG): for small models the JSON format of tool schemas is itself the dominant failure driver (accuracy collapses to 0–49% at >15 tools); deterministically rendering the same schemas as plain text restores Phi-4 14B 0→84.4% at 20 tools and 90.3% at 50; mechanism is format translation, not token compression (R² 0.88→0.03) — JSON, the production standard, is measurably the worst representation for small-model tool use.
  links: 308c11e281ffbca4, 1d39a325632460a2, 39c9cdfc179c58ba, 5f7aa50430ec256f, 998dc6a4eeebfd10
- `467e731dc67aed05` [general] A claim that a failure was recovered is only as good as its verifier: this repo's AGENTS.md requires run verify to actually execute the declared verify_command before an outcome is trusted — no external source substitutes for executing a recovery path on your own system.
  links: 20775a40371d1e55, 7fe943345da11d30, ac9b554f4139828d, abee484fca1be8a6, 8af8f2689671ac0a
- `486c92d91a5c5d33` [general] mcp-hub documents no support/maintenance policy for older major versions (no LTS window, no deprecation/end-of-life statement), no prerelease/RC/nightly policy (and no such versions published), and no minimum-Node-version support commitment beyond the README runtime requirement 'Node.js >= 18.0.0'; the absence of a support-window policy is itself a finding, not a claim about what the policy is.
  links: 036d4210a72a6375, a4b2703c22963a98, d479456f357031ec, 9a3185f8c42ed664, f8fc8c1992134bc3
- `4f5ffa664297decb` [general] BFCL robustness (arXiv:2504.00914): function-calling selection and slot-filling degrade when the toolkit grows from mean 2.7 to 5.6 tools: Llama-3.1-70B 0.965→0.825, Claude-3.5-Sonnet 0.915→0.845, gpt-4o-mini 0.925→0.765. 70–90% of errors in the meaning-preserving-rephrasing condition are parameter-value mismatches (an eval artifact). Adding tools occasionally made models 'repair' a previously failed call — stochastic flip-flop relevant to retry-loop design (a retry with perturbed context can both fix and break a call).
  links: 2df44a2e25263275, a4951002397ee060, 4d082f928948911f, f15a4d58c0bb1a4a, b25f3d9404e1c402
- `554b0217976296b3` [general] DeepVerifier DRA failure taxonomy (Wan et al., CUHK/Tencent; arXiv 2601.15808, ACL 2026 Findings) defines 5 major classes and 13 sub-classes built to drive rubric-based verification: Problem Understanding, Finding Sources (dominant — e.g., consulting wrong evidence, generic searches), Reasoning (premature conclusions, misinterpretation, hallucinated/overconfident claims), Action Errors, Max Step Reached; 13 sub-class names shown in Fig. 3 were not all reproduced in the fetched text.
  links: 8479b0749854fe46, f4b23532d8f51878, bacb0e234ea35e04, 9ef58d49f849ae77, add1799958c47c93
- `56ffb2187d17cbe9` [general] Zep/Graphiti dynamic community extension "gradually diverge[s]" from a full label-propagation run, so "periodic community refreshes remain necessary"; the dynamic path exists because it "significantly reduces latency and LLM inference costs" [S5] §2.3; design rationale is continuous incremental updates for cost/latency, periodic batch recompute for quality.
  links: a1cb6948c6bd72ed, ecda50a3261745b3, 76d2c318013e0da9, 4c4453fd5125f78d, 4cf12f9a8fbae069
- `582d97a2bb8bd903` [general] HippoRAG (Gutiérrez et al., NeurIPS 2024, arXiv:2405.14831) reports on long-document QA (MuSiQue, 2WikiMultiHopQA, HotpotQA) that full-context baseline accuracy 'drops' as document length grows while the retrieval-based approach keeps accuracy higher at a fraction of the tokens. The reported numbers are accuracy figures (HippoRAG ~12-17% on MuSiQue vs SOTA ~35%+), not token-savings figures; cost reduction is stated qualitatively as 'more efficient'. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.14831
  links: e18bd137f36455e4, e368dbd927efdfc0, 86494d2770f9e1c6, 7d97975df00109c5, 15116661844082ce
- `59735e32b612df1a` [general] BM25 underperforms dense retrievers on agent-memory fact recall: LongMemEval retriever ablation (Value=Session, K=V) gives BM25 Recall@5=0.634 / NDCG@5=0.516 vs Contriever 0.723/0.634 vs Stella V5 1.5B 0.720/0.594 (Wu et al., ICLR 2025). Source: arXiv:2410.10813v2, Table 9, Appendix E.2.
  links: 50662f026de1acfe, 2c6fb8ddf6a41794, 999b1d884e08eb37, 68944cdb3db0a2fc, 832a7c538944c10a
- `70d2e0f66bef8b38` [general] Zep/Graphiti (arXiv:2501.13956 §2.2.3) uses a bi-temporal model: timeline T (when a fact holds) and T' (transactional ingestion), each edge storing t_valid, t_invalid, t'_created, t'_expired; an LLM compares new edges against semantically related existing edges for contradictions and invalidates temporally overlapping affected edges by setting t_invalid to the invalidating edge's t_valid; Graphiti prioritizes new information; retrieval returns the t_valid/t_invalid range so stale-but-historical facts remain representable.
  links: 92823ba871a2eaae, b6fef0270ed8d28d, ca509f76052d6818, 5f22c03352903571, e286837b45d310e8

- `76649269e1ba035d` [general] Seccomp userspace notification (SECCOMP_RET_USER_NOTIF) reintroduces TOCTOU and is explicitly not a security mechanism: 'the seccomp user-space notification mechanism can not be used to implement a security policy'; SECCOMP_USER_NOTIF_FLAG_CONTINUE has a time-of-check/time-of-use race because the target can rewrite syscall arguments while blocked; a notifier 'can be bypassed if the existing filters allow the use of seccomp(2) or prctl(2) to install a filter' with higher-precedence action.
  links: d71aa36a9e81bc8c, a68feba40bd78382, 9e867896987f3592, a0ae947f3627d7ca, 7b032b76d2592172
- `7d97975df00109c5` [general] LLMLingua (Jiang et al., Microsoft, EMNLP 2023, arXiv:2310.05736) reports coarse-to-fine prompt compression achieving a ratio up to 20x with minimal performance loss (budgets 1x/3x/5x/10x/20x), with measured savings in compressed token count and FLOPs reduction. It targets whole prompts (demonstrations, instructions, chain-of-thought), not agent memory. Source: https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.05736

- `7e96686c5bcb8461` [general] Three-tier task-phase taxonomy (Lu, Li, Huo; arXiv 2508.13143, ASE 2025 NIER) aligns agent failures to task phases: planning errors, task execution issues, incorrect response generation; benchmarked on 34 programmable tasks with 3 open-source agent frameworks x 2 LLM backbones, ~50% observed task completion, causes induced from in-depth failure analysis.

- `7fcceed280fcc7b0` [general] LangGraph separates full snapshots from finer-grained writes: per-node pending writes are written durably within a super-step so successful nodes are not re-run on resume, while time-travel/replay resumes only at full super-step checkpoints — snapshot-restore plus per-task delta.

- `7fe943345da11d30` [evidence-status] mini-agi: the run's `outcome.achieved` is the run's OWN claim until `mini-agi run verify <run.json>` confirms it; ADR-0011 records the prior state as 'The eval harness trusts run.json's self-reported outcome — nothing runs the work's own gate' and makes verification additive so a run without a verifier stays `unverified` with the report saying so explicitly.

- `804ffb8a7c26c4aa` [general] Bazel's `flaky` test attribute executes a test up to three times, marking it failed only if it fails every time; default False (single execution). Its use is generally discouraged. (Bazel Build Encyclopedia)

- `81601e15dda67071` [general] The Cargo Book's CI chapter is the closest thing to an official CI gate spec: build+test on a channel matrix; warnings gate via CARGO_BUILD_WARNINGS=deny; a latest-deps job marked continue-on-error; a rust-version verification job; and explicit trade-off notes (exhaustiveness vs turnaround, CI cost, toolchain-upgrade churn).

- `8f32a4d8dcbf2ca5` [general] Claude Code has four settings scopes (managed/user/project/local) with fixed precedence; managed settings cannot be overridden by other scopes; project .claude/settings.json loads only from the starting directory; a workspace-trust dialog gates project allow rules; MCP servers are scoped per scope (.mcp.json for project, ~/.claude.json for user/local).

- `9eba1c8e9d2d65b7` [general] State handle hijacking: MCP is stateless and servers mint state handles returned as tool arguments; possession of a state handle MUST NOT be treated as authentication — per-request authorization, user-bound handles (<user_id>:<handle>), secure non-deterministic handles, and expiry are required (Security Best Practices).
  links: c81957cf3aac4c06, af0d9ca54cec9c18, 57ed22f30f0c6760, e4344628bee35e4c, 9a9c35587bb37962
- `9ef58d49f849ae77` [general] Tool-agent parameter-filling taxonomy (Xiong et al.; arXiv 2507.15296, 'Butterfly Effects in Toolchains') defines five failure categories derived from the invocation chain of a mainstream tool agent; parameter-name-hallucination failures reportedly stem chiefly from LLM limits, other categories trace to input sources; measured with 15 input perturbation methods correlated against failure categories. Individual category names are not enumerated in the abstract and could not be verified (arXiv HTML 404, PDF unreadable).

- `9fd0d62f3cea36ac` [general] DMR (Deep Memory Retrieval) is a task introduced in the MemGPT paper, built on the Multi-Session Chat (MSC) dataset; the agent is asked a question referring back to a prior conversation with a very narrow expected answer range, with QA pairs generated by a separate LLM instructed to write questions answerable only from the old chat log and not contained in persona information.
  links: 30d49ce68046cd2f, 672b4912c42144b2, 9cd6a6c67868558e, 2bc3ff3548b14a6f, 888b4004f537880f
- `a1cb6948c6bd72ed` [general] No system in the surveyed literature uses a literal "nightly cron"; "nightly" is approximated by daily-frequency batch reflection (Generative Agents) and idle-time scheduled consolidation (Letta sleep-time compute), while continuous background consolidation is approximated by per-event incremental ingestion (Zep/Graphiti, Mem0, A-MEM).
  links: 75149e672ee23e31, 56ffb2187d17cbe9, ecda50a3261745b3, 8718ee8dce4540b4, 9cc983109ba5b4f3
- `a2d22fc6a58aa061` [repair] xmemory (system report): judge-in-the-loop configuration reached 90.42% object-level and 62.67% output accuracy, above all tested frontier structured-output baselines; end-to-end memory benchmark 97.10% F1 vs 80.16–87.24% for third-party baselines; 95.2% on an application task — an LLM-judge + retry loop raises whole-output exactness from ~34–44% to ~63%, closing part of the compounding gap but plateauing well below field-level saturation.
  links: 97b9c2be82edddfc, f8edba98b002ec5d, 86be403af727cc0f, d84e1b455ff7763a, 8df10858c5595438
- `a40757229890299e` [general] Temporal's durable execution is event sourcing in disguise: the workflow sends Commands mapped to Events persisted to a complete durable log; on worker crash, the worker uses the Event History to replay the code and recreate the workflow state as it was immediately before the crash.
  links: ecca3611145516e6, 1c641abef370327a, a66abf4c0c1f97cf, 6665ad2250b0bf6a, 8baa3b2f82d7faa6
- `aa17692c22a78738` [general] Interaction-Centric Taxonomy (Raj, Gupta, Mahmoud et al., Scale AI; arXiv 2607.28802, July 2026) defines 41 LLM-agent failure modes, each assigned to an interaction edge between two components and a fault side (model vs. other); components are Model, Owner, Grader, Third party, Context, Memory, Tool, Local env., External env. grouped into three families (User, Harness, Environment); 36 modes are model-side, 5 are component-side.
  links: f7a62764ca74d0e9, 38956b8103d9e760, 02a02d256e8ffada, d897024c3f240a4b, c1a0b768075c2467
- `ab49a330f50c49ef` [general] Huang et al. (2024, ICLR) tested intrinsic self-correction (no external feedback) on reasoning: models 'struggle to self-correct their responses without external feedback, and at times, their performance even degrades after self-correction' (arXiv:2310.01798).
  links: b66006c1f5cda644, 6b4813b0dee8f914, 33f44ff98f2913eb, 635c8cbfd002fa86, f103d9ef613d250f
- `b91eeab1a9452dee` [general] Candidate adoption (1): keep run.json authoritative but consider a unified ledger summary index that is git-commit-friendly and queryable (status already partially does this); Beads is a single version-controlled DB while mini-agi splits state across checkpoints.log + run.json + canonical.
  links: a602df8d9b46c6b5, 0ff9b686f9e72fc3, 23db60932cbf376c, f54b0ed68eef1372, b4ee49de2637dd9e
- `c3484bbb30701da2` [general] Local server compromise mitigations: mandatory pre-execution consent showing the exact untruncated command, sandboxing, least privilege, warning on dangerous patterns (sudo, rm -rf, network ops); prefer stdio or gate HTTP transports behind tokens / restricted unix sockets (Security Best Practices).
  links: 3f8680deb624fd36, c81957cf3aac4c06, 57ed22f30f0c6760, 9a9c35587bb37962, c83ae8c7cde41572
- `cfddc08e47e6dcf7` [general] Oracle real-time materialized views avoid refreshing entirely: the optimizer writes a query combining existing MV rows with changes recorded in log files since the last refresh (on-query computation).

- `cfe795ab8c642a7e` [repair] Palabrita (reported): authors' explicit compounding arithmetic — 'for n fields with independent violation probability p, expected success is (1−p)^n; reducing from 5 to 2 fields approximately doubled the success rate'; implication: at SLM scale measured structured-output reliability is dramatically lower than frontier models (0–50% constraint compliance without heavy engineering).
  links: 4cb71d0255cccf49, 308c11e281ffbca4, 2512869e54f72f96, 55dfb567f49f3e98, f4596f48ade2d79f

- `d39c4dd366a95d2e` [general] Verdict (established): the four systems converge on one pattern — a single kernel binary whose identity, memory, and enforcement are all resolved per-repo from a scope stack (global/user, project, nested subdirectory), with git root as the identity anchor and a trust gate on project-loaded content; MCP is the only cross-repo mechanism and is stateless per-request with no memory or eval semantics.
  links: 1691fdd86c2e2438, b4ee49de2637dd9e, 2a637dd506865c01, fac488c6012dc0f4, e8df321a0ca42231
- `d6354b5974db8518` [general] Develocity considers a test flaky if it fails and then succeeds within the same Gradle task or Maven goal execution, marking such tests FLAKY in build scans; retry is required to detect this, requires no code changes, applies to the whole suite, and enables proactive detection of newly introduced flaky tests. The Tests Dashboard ranks the most severe flaky tests and plots trends over time to confirm fixes.
  links: 804ffb8a7c26c4aa, 3880083953a8f0d3, 24f290b647ef095e, af500e05f86d5f38, 56702ea84de54b43
- `d6482a9be6ea492f` [general] opencode rule discovery order: local AGENTS.md traversing up from cwd, then global ~/.config/opencode/AGENTS.md, then ~/.claude/CLAUDE.md (Claude Code compat fallback); project config resolves by looking in the current directory then traversing up to the nearest Git directory; config files are merged, later layers override conflicting keys only.

- `e18bd137f36455e4` [general] Entity/graph retrieval gives the biggest measured gains on multi-hop fact integration: HippoRAG R@5 on 2WikiMultiHopQA BM25 61.9 / ColBERTv2 68.2 / HippoRAG 89.1 (+20 points); on MuSiQue 41.2/49.2/51.9 (~+3); QA F1 2Wiki 43.3→59.5 (+16.2), MuSiQue 26.4→29.8. - `edac6d9654eefceb` [ai-auditability] Opinion: the three mechanisms answer different audit questions — decision logs answer what was decided and why (rationale + when/who/what fields); evidence chains answer what produced this result and can it be re-verified (attribution + derivation + digests, tamper-evident); approval records answer who authorized it (a human gate persisted as a record).

- `f0afcf1604a978a8` [repair] IBM CW (system report): redesigned piecewise pipeline (v2: deterministic early Planner → retrieve evidence → generate workflow variables → base blocks → nested blocks separately, then programmatic-fix step before LLM JSON-Patch correction, ≤2 retry rounds) raised structural success to 74.1–97.8% with four of six models >90% (zero under v1) — decomposing the generation task was a more reliable fix for complex-schema JSON than any single-model choice, taking the worst v1 model (33.2%) past the best v1 model's ceiling.

- `f4cbdf553ae9377e` [repair] Tool-Veritas (Vaghasiya et al.): deterministic-first scoring (state gates + restricted LLM fallback, bounded repair window) raised expert agreement to 95.5% (401/420) vs 69–90% for the audited families, with all 19 disagreements being stricter (false negatives, no unsupported passes) — the recommended direction for repair-loop-relevant evaluation; implication: BFCL V4's quoted overall/multi-turn percentages (e.g., 77.47% top model) carry an ~20% evaluator-error band and should be read as relative rankings, not absolute success rates.

- `f6c08ef82bdb0411` [repair] Agentic fault examples (Shah et al.): LLM Integration faults include invalid JSON responses (MetaGPT producing invalid JSON when its RAG module misinteracted with the Ollama provider); API Parameter Mismatch includes tool-calling failures from 'null values where structured parameters were expected' (SWE-agent); token-handling faults from different tokenizers between framework and provider causing context overflow; association-rule mining shows faults 'do not fail transparently — weak error handling and limited logging obscure their underlying causes' (e.g., auth failures ← token-refresh mishandling, lift 181.5; wrong time values ← datetime-conversion errors, lift 121.0).
  links: f3b28a55a63ea68b, 4d082f928948911f, b023dfa34d7b6603, e5a6d2ccb1b2bd7a, ef69e42494b930cc
- `f7e3fa39e01a0051` [general] ExtractBench (arXiv:2602.12247) — the primary source behind the 4.6% figure: 35 PDFs, 5 domains, 12,867 evaluatable fields. Aggregate field-level pass rate 4.6% (844/18,516 across 6 frontier models); best model (Gemini 3 Flash) 6.9%; valid-JSON rate 51% with valid-only field accuracy 72.9% (valid≠correct even when valid). 0% valid output on a 369-field SEC 10-K/Q schema across all models (0/2,583); excluding 10-K/Q pass rate is 28.0%. Schema breadth is the dominant predictor, output volume the binding constraint. Failure modes: empty response 40%, trailing commas 30% (Claude/Gemini-only), PDF page limit 19%, truncation ~9%.

- `fe22d29d30fbcaf7` [general] Anthropic enterprise skills gates: pre-deployment approval gates for five dimensions (triggering accuracy, isolation, coexistence, instruction following, output quality); required eval suites of 3–5 queries per skill (should-trigger/should-not-trigger/ambiguous); separation of duties (authors ≠ reviewers); risk-tier vetting (code execution, instruction manipulation, MCP references, network calls, hardcoded credentials); integrity checksums verified at deploy time and signed commits; recall capped at max 8 skills per request to protect trigger accuracy.

The cascade is a length-3 list scored via a DistilBERT regressor. (FrugalGPT, arXiv 2305.05176, §3-4)

- `24f290b647ef095e` [general] Kubernetes quarantines a single test by adding [Flaky] to its test name; most CI jobs exclude these tests, which run only in explicitly flaky jobs (e.g. the gci-gce-flaky job). Quarantining a presubmit test requires a release-milestone issue labeled priority/critical-urgent, lifecycle/frozen, and kind/flake, with the owning SIG expected to fix and reintroduce or delete the test. Whole-suite quarantine uses [Feature:Foo] names plus dedicated jobs, which merge-blocking suites avoid unless proven non-flaky.

- `49e58f71d2bdd715` [evidence-status] Anthropic Claude Code documents enforcement tiers for checks: in-prompt iteration; `/goal` condition re-checked by a separate evaluator every turn; a Stop hook that runs the check as a script and blocks the turn from ending until it passes (overridden after 8 consecutive blocks); and a verification subagent where a fresh model tries to refute the result so the worker isn't grading itself.

This is documented pain from the flat-file model, not a measured comparison.

Seccomp BPF is CONFIG-gated (CONFIG_SECCOMP_FILTER) and has per-arch and kernel-version surface (e.g. the x32 nr fix in 5.4).

(Kadavath et al., Anthropic, arXiv 2207.05221; verified abstract)

(Maharana et al., arXiv:2402.17753)

- `6bf04b5388b27261` [general] BFCL V3 (UC Berkeley) defines the reference multi-turn function-calling design: 800 human-labeled cases, state-based + response-based subset-matched evaluation (a call is correct only if it passes both in all turns), turn force-terminated after 20 steps ('stuck in a loop' guard). The design rewards error recovery: a trajectory that fails then retries with corrected parameters passes — retry-after-error-feedback is treated as correct behavior. Full V3 leaderboard scale: 4,441 entries.

- `83f1964c6217fb0d` [general] ReAct (Yao et al., 2023), interleaving sparse 'thought' traces with environment actions, outperformed imitation and RL baselines by +34% (ALFWorld) and +10% (WebShop) absolute success rate; on WebShop ReAct's 40.0% SR beat Act (30.1%), IL (29.1%), and IL+RL (28.7%). Grounding reasoning in a Wikipedia API cut hallucination: among failed HotpotQA trajectories, hallucination was the cause in 56% of CoT cases vs 0% for ReAct (arXiv:2210.03629).

- `8479b0749854fe46` [general] MAST (Cemri et al., UC Berkeley; arXiv 2503.13657) defines 14 LLM-agent failure modes in 3 categories: FC1 System Design Issues (disobey task spec 11.8%, disobey role spec 1.5%, step repetition 15.7%, loss of conversation history 2.8%, unaware of stopping conditions 12.4%), FC2 Inter-Agent Misalignment (conversation reset 2.2%, fail to ask for clarification 6.8%, task derailment 7.4%, information withholding 0.85%, ignored other agent's input 1.9%, reasoning-action mismatch 13.2%), FC3 Task Verification (premature termination 6.2%, no/incomplete verification 8.2%, incorrect verification 9.1%).
  links: 38956b8103d9e760, 02a02d256e8ffada, d897024c3f240a4b, 554b0217976296b3
- `8de41cd967fdda17` [general] DMR/MSC setup: MSC conversations have five sessions of ~12 messages, plus a new session 6 containing one QA pair; Zep reports DMR as a 500-conversation subset of MSC. Metrics are ROUGE-L plus an LLM judge scoring CORRECT/WRONG. Reported accuracies: GPT-3.5 Turbo 38.7% -> MemGPT 66.9%; GPT-4 32.1% -> 92.5%; GPT-4 Turbo 35.3% -> 93.4%.
  links: 30d49ce68046cd2f, d4d8a0005c2c4c6a, ca79380b2a16a6e8, 15116661844082ce
- `8eff920971cc7319` [general] Claude Code hooks fire per session (`SessionStart`/`SessionEnd`), per turn (`UserPromptSubmit`, `Stop`), and on every tool call (`PreToolUse`, `PostToolUse`, `PostToolUseFailure`, plus `PermissionRequest`/`PermissionDenied`); handlers can be shell commands, HTTP endpoints, MCP tools, LLM prompts, or subagents; `matcher` (tool name/regex) + `if` (permission-rule syntax) filter them; MCP tools match as `mcp__<server>__<tool>`; hooks can return `permissionDecision: 'deny'` to block a call; hooks live in settings files, managed policy, plugin `hooks/hooks.json`, or skill/agent frontmatter; `allowManagedHooksOnly` lets enterprises suppress user/project/plugin hooks.
  links: 0d0774d31bed17b3, 4ba0795d7351a698, 900ff2712391e9e4, 0cddb3ab4b575e9e
- `900ff2712391e9e4` [general] Codex enforcement uses sandbox modes, approval policies, and allow_managed_hooks_only (ignores user/project/session hooks, keeps managed ones), which is honored only in requirements.toml, i.e., the org-owned layer.
  links: 8eff920971cc7319, a2ba28d2061e0152, 9a41828e542b1154, 4ba0795d7351a698
- `97b9c2be82edddfc` [general] Zep, M3-Embedding, and HippoRAG benchmark numbers are author-reported; Zep is the vendor of the tested product. 2026 preprint headline numbers (AgentIR, TiMem) were verified only at abstract level and could not be independently corroborated; several look implausibly high next to peer-reviewed baselines.
